a\in A
,

|\langle b^{\epsilon,P}(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi;\rho\rangle|\leq C(1+|a|^{2})\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}\left\|\rho\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1})^{*}}.

Therefore, if
\nu
belongs to
\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{2}}(A)
, the quantity
\int_{A}|\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi;\rho\rangle|d|\nu|(a)
is finite and we can then use Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem to pass to the limit in the left-hand side of (
7.9
) to get

\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\lim_{P\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{A}\langle b^{\epsilon,P}(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi;\rho\rangle d\nu(a)=\int_{A}\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi;\rho\rangle d\nu(a).

We now address the limit in the right-hand side of
(
7.9
). By the same argument as above,

\displaystyle|\langle b^{\epsilon,P}(\cdot,\nu)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi;\rho\rangle-\langle b(\cdot,\nu)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi;\rho\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|b^{\epsilon,P}(\cdot,\nu)-b(\cdot,\nu)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}\left\|\rho\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{A}\left\|b^{\epsilon,P}(\cdot,a)-b(\cdot,a)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}d|\nu|(a).

In order to show that the right-hand side tends to
0
,
we use Lemma
7.2
to infer that, for every
a\in A
,

\lim_{\epsilon\rightarrow 0}\lim_{P\rightarrow+\infty}\left\|b^{\epsilon,P}(\cdot,a)-b(\cdot,a)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}=0.

We also observe that, for a new value of the constant
C>0
(which is independent of
\epsilon>0
and
P\geq 1
),

\left\|b^{\epsilon,P}(\cdot,a)-b(\cdot,a)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}\leq C(1+|a|^{2}).

We can apply once again Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem to conclude that (
7.5
) holds true.
∎

We also need the following lemma in order to establish the measurability property stated in the first item of Proposition

7.1
:

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
\varphi:[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a function such that
\nabla_{x}\varphi\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})\cap\mathcal{L}^{\infty}([t_{0},T],\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1})
, and let
\rho\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1})^{*})
with
\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}_{1}^{2},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}_{2}^{1}}\leq 1}\langle\varphi;\rho_{t}\rangle
finite.
Then, for any
a\in A
, the map
t\mapsto\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle
is continuous. Moreover, for all
t_{1}<t_{2}\in[t_{0},T]
, we have the following inequality:

\displaystyle|\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{2}};\rho_{t_{2}}\rangle-\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{1}};\rho_{t_{1}}\rangle|\leq C(1+|a|^{3})

\displaystyle\times\Bigl(\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}}+\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}_{1}^{2},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}_{2}^{1}}\leq 1}\langle\varphi;\rho_{t}\rangle\Bigr)\Bigl(\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{2}}-\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{1}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}+\left\|\rho_{t_{2}}-\rho_{t_{1}}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1})^{*}}\Bigr).

Proof.

With the same notation as in the statement,
we have, for any
a\in A
,

\displaystyle|\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{2}};\rho_{t_{2}}\rangle-\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{1}};\rho_{t_{1}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}(\varphi_{t_{2}}-\varphi_{t_{1}});\rho_{t_{2}}\rangle|+|\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{1}};\rho_{t_{2}}-\rho_{t_{1}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\rho_{t_{2}}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})^{*}}\left\|b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}(\varphi_{t_{2}}-\varphi_{t_{1}})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}+\left\|b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{1}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}}\left\|\rho_{t_{2}}-\rho_{t_{1}}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1})^{*}}.

And then, we complete the proof by noticing from
Assumption (
Regularity
)
that

\begin{split}&\left\|b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}(\varphi_{t_{2}}-\varphi_{t_{1}})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\leq C(1+|a|^{2})\left\|\nabla_{x}(\varphi_{t_{2}}-\varphi_{t_{1}})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}},\\
&\left\|b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{1}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}}\leq C(1+|a|^{3})\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{1}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}},\end{split}

for
a\in A
.
∎

We can finally finish the Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

The first item in the statement follows from Lemma

7.5
.
It only remains to prove point (
2
).
We let
\tau:=T-t_{0}
and, for all
N\geq 1
and
1\leq i\leq N
,
t_{i}^{N}:=t_{0}+\frac{i-1}{N}\tau
, so that
[t_{0},T]
can be decomposed into
\sqcup_{i=1}^{N-1}[t_{i}^{N},t_{i+1}^{N}[\sqcup[t_{N}^{N},T].

We first claim that, as a consequence of the measurability of the
map
t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto(1+|a|^{2})^{-1}\nu_{t}\in{\mathcal{M}}(A)
, the map

t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto\int_{A}\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle d\nu_{t}(a)
is measurable
for each fixed
pair
(N,i)
, with
N\geq 1
and
i\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
. Indeed,
we know that
the map

t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{M}(A)
is measurable.
Therefore, for any
R\geq 1
, the map

t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto\int_{\{|a|\leq R\}}\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle d\nu_{t}(a)

is measurable.
Since
\bm{\nu}
belongs to
\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
,
the set
\{t\in[t_{0},T]:\int_{A}(1+|a|^{4})d\nu_{t}(a)<\infty\}
is a Borel subset of
[t_{0},T]
and has full Lebesgue measure.
In particular, the map
t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto{\mathbf{1}}_{E}(t)\int_{\{|a|\leq R\}}\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle d\nu_{t}(a)
is measurable.
Since the function
a\in A\mapsto\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle
is measurable with quadratic growth (uniformly in
t
), we can easily let
R
tend to
+\infty

and deduce that the map

t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto{\mathbf{1}}_{E}(t)\int_{A}\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle d\nu_{t}(a)
is measurable, which suffices to construct a measurable version
of
t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto\int_{A}\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle d\nu_{t}(a)
.

For
(N,i)
fixed as above and for
E
also defined as above, we observe from Lemma
7.5
that
t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto{\mathbf{1}}_{E}(t)\int_{A}\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle d\nu_{t}(a)
coincides with
t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto{\mathbf{1}}_{E}(t)\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle
.
We deduce that, for all
t\in E
,

(7.10)

\begin{split}&\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle\mathbf{1}_{[t_{i}^{N},t_{i+1}^{N}[}(t)+\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{N}^{N}};\rho_{t_{N}^{N}}\rangle\mathbf{1}_{[t_{N}^{N},t_{N+1}^{N}]}(t)\\
&=\int_{A}\Bigl\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle\mathbf{1}_{[t_{i}^{N},t_{i+1}^{N}[}(t)+\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{N}^{N}};\rho_{t_{N}^{N}}\rangle\mathbf{1}_{[t_{N}^{N},t_{N+1}^{N}]}(t)\Bigr\}d\nu_{t}(a).\end{split}

By the previous paragraph, the function

{\bm{\beta}}^{N}:t\mapsto{\mathbf{1}}_{E}(t)\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{i}^{N}};\rho_{t_{i}^{N}}\rangle\mathbf{1}_{[t_{i}^{N},t_{i+1}^{N}[}(t)+\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{N}^{N}};\rho_{t_{N}^{N}}\rangle\mathbf{1}_{[t_{N}^{N},t_{N+1}^{N}]}(t)\biggr)

is measurable.
The goal is then to pass to the limit as
N
tends to
+\infty
.
By Lemma
7.4
,
we have, for all
t\in E
,

\displaystyle\bigl|{\bm{\beta}}^{N}(t)-\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\biggl(\int_{A}(1+|a|^{3})d\nu_{t}(a)\biggr)\Bigl(\sup_{s\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{s}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}}+\sup_{s\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\rho_{s}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})^{*}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt\times\sup_{|s-r|\leq\tau/N}\Bigl(\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{r}-\nabla_{x}\varphi_{s}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}+\left\|\rho_{r}-\rho_{s}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1})^{*}}\Bigr).

By definition of
E
, the right-hand side tends to
0
as
N
tends to
+\infty
. We deduce that
the function
{\bm{\beta}}:t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto{\mathbf{1}}_{E}(t)\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle
is measurable as point-wise limit of measurable functions. Passing to
the limit in
(
7.10
) (thanks again to
Lemma
7.4
),
we see that

{\bm{\beta}}

coincides with
t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto{\mathbf{1}}_{E}(t)\int_{A}\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle d\nu_{t}(a)
. This proves the first part in item (2) of the statement.

Finally, for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
we have, by assumption on
b
,

|\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle|\leq C_{b}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})^{*}}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\nu_{t}|(a)

for some
C_{b}>0
depending only on
b
. Estimate (
7.2
) follows after integrating in
t
.
∎

Before ending this section we add a similar result about the differentiability of the map
a\mapsto\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi;\rho\bigr\rangle
.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\rho
be an element of
(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}
such that
\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}\leq 1}}\langle\varphi;\rho\rangle
is finite and let
\varphi:\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a function such that
\nabla_{x}\varphi
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}
. Then, the map
a\in A\mapsto\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi;\rho\rangle
is differentiable, and its gradient is given by
a\mapsto\langle\nabla_{a}[b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi];\rho\rangle.

Proof.

Let
f(x,y,a):=b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi(x,y)
, for
(x,y,a)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}}\times A
. Then, by assumptions on
b
, for every
a_{0}\in A
we have, by Taylor’s expansion

f(x,y,a)=f(x,y,a_{0})+(a-a_{0})\cdot\nabla_{a}f(x,y,a_{0})+\epsilon(x,y,a),

where

\epsilon(x,y,a)=\biggl(\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\nabla^{2}_{a}f(x,y,a_{0}+t(a-a_{0}))dt\biggr)(a-a_{0})\cdot(a-a_{0}).

Using the properties of
b
in
Assumption (
Regularity
)
,
we see that
\epsilon

satisfies

\lim_{a\rightarrow a_{0}}\frac{\left\|\epsilon(\cdot,a)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}}{|a-a_{0}|}=0.

Observing that for all
a\in A
,
the three functions
(x,y)\mapsto\nabla_{x}\varphi(x,y)
,
(x,y)\mapsto b(x,a)
and
(x,y)\mapsto\nabla_{a}b(x,a)
belong to
\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}
, we easily deduce that, for any

a\in A
,

(x,y)\mapsto f(x,y,a)
and

(x,y)\mapsto\nabla_{a}f(x,y,a)

belong to
{\mathcal{C}}^{2}_{2}
.
Writing

\epsilon(x,y,a)=f(x,y,a)-f(x,y,a_{0})-(a-a_{0})\cdot\nabla_{a}f(x,y,a_{0})
,
for
(x,y,a)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}}\times A
,
we deduce that, for any
a\in A
,

\epsilon(\cdot,a)
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}
. By linearity of the duality bracket we have

\langle f(\cdot,a);\rho\rangle=\langle f(\cdot,a_{0});\rho\rangle+(a-a_{0})\cdot\langle\nabla_{a}f(\cdot,a_{0});\rho\rangle+\langle\epsilon(\cdot,a);\rho\rangle

and the remainder can be estimated by

\limsup_{a\rightarrow a_{0}}\frac{|\langle\epsilon(\cdot,a);\rho\rangle|}{|a-a_{0}|}\leq\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}_{2}^{2},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq 1}\langle\varphi;\rho\rangle\times\lim_{a\rightarrow a_{0}}\frac{\left\|\epsilon(\cdot,a)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}}{|a-a_{0}|}=0,

which completes the proof.
∎

Now we turn to the well-posedness of the linearized equation
for
\bm{\rho}
, see
(
7.1
). We recall that the notion of solution is given in Section
2.4
.

7.1.2.
Existence

We continue the analysis with the following lemma on the stability
properties of the continuity equation (
2.8
):

Lemma 7.6
.

Take
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
, for
p\in[2,3]
,
and
\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
. For any
\lambda\in[-1,1]
, let
\bm{\nu}^{\lambda}:=\bm{\nu}+\lambda\bm{\eta}
, and
\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}
be the solution to

(7.11)

\partial_{t}\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}+\operatorname{div}_{x}\bigl(b(x,\nu^{\lambda}_{t})\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr)=0\quad\mbox{in }(t_{0},T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\gamma_{t_{0}}=\gamma_{0}.

For
\lambda\neq 0,
we define
\bm{\delta\gamma}^{\lambda}:=\lambda^{-1}[\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}-\bm{\gamma}^{0}].
Then, there exists a non-decreasing function
\Lambda:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
, independent of
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})
,
\bm{\nu}
and
\bm{\eta}
, such that

(7.12)

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda\in[-1,1]\setminus\{0\}}\Bigl\{\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p})^{*}}+\sup_{t_{1}\neq t_{2}\in[t_{0},T]}\frac{\left\|\delta\gamma_{t_{2}}^{\lambda}-\delta\gamma_{t_{1}}^{\lambda}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*}}}{\sqrt{|t_{2}-t_{1}|}}\Bigr\}\leq\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\lambda\bm{\eta},\gamma_{0}}\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})},

(7.13)

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda_{1}\neq\lambda_{2}\in[-1,1]\setminus\{0\}}\frac{1}{|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*}}\leq\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta},\gamma_{0}}\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})},

where, for simplicity,
we used
\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\lambda\bm{\eta},\gamma_{0}}

(with
\lambda
being equal to 1 in
(
7.13
))
as a short-hand notation:

(7.14)

\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\lambda\bm{\eta},\gamma_{0}}:=\Lambda\biggl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}+|\lambda|\,\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})^{p/2}d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\biggr).

Proof.

Step 1.
By subtracting the two equations (
7.11
) for the cases
\lambda\neq 0
and
\lambda=0
, and then dividing by
\lambda
, we obtain that
\bm{\delta\gamma}^{\lambda}
satisfies the equation

(7.15)

\partial_{t}\delta\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}+\operatorname{div}_{x}\bigl(b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda})\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr)=-\operatorname{div}_{x}\bigl(b(x,\eta_{t})\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}\bigr)\quad\mbox{ in }(t_{0},T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\delta\gamma_{t_{0}}^{\lambda}=0.

By Lemma
A.10
in Appendix
A.1
,
\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}
and
\bm{\gamma}
have
bounded
p
-moments, uniformly in
t\in[t_{0},T]
. Using a standard approximation argument, this shows that the equation above can be tested against any function
\varphi:[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that
\varphi,\partial_{t}\varphi,\nabla_{x}\varphi
are continuous
and bounded, up to a multiplicative constant, by
(1+|x|^{p}+|y|^{p})
, uniformly in

t\in[t_{0},T]
.

Precisely, the test functions
\varphi
are chosen
according to the following duality principle. For all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
\lambda\in[-1,1]
, we call
\bm{\nu}^{\lambda,n}
the approximation of
\bm{\nu}^{\lambda}
given by Lemma
A.9
.
Then, for
t_{1}\in(t_{0},T]
and
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}
,
we let

\varphi^{\lambda,n}:[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be the solution to

(7.16)

-\partial_{t}\varphi^{\lambda,n}_{t}-b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda,n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi^{\lambda,n}_{t}=0\quad\mbox{ in }[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\varphi^{\lambda,n}_{t_{1}}=\phi\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},

as given by Lemma
A.6
.

By
combining
Lemmas

A.3

and

A.6
,
we can find a non-decreasing function
\Lambda
, independent of

t_{0}
,
\bm{\nu}
and

\phi
, such that

(7.17)

\sup_{\lambda\in[-1,1]}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|\varphi_{t}^{\lambda,n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}+|\lambda|\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}}.

Step 2.
Inserting the above bound in
(
7.16
), and using the growth assumption on
b

(as stated in
Assumption (
Regularity
)
),
this implies that
\|\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}^{\lambda,n}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{p}_{b}}

is bounded. In particular,
\varphi^{\lambda,n}
is an admissible test function for (
7.15
), and we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\bigl[\delta\gamma_{t_{1}}^{\lambda}\bigr](x,y)

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\bigl\{\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}^{\lambda,n}(x,y)+b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\lambda,n}(x,y)\bigr\}d\bigl[\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr](x,y)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\lambda,n}(x,y)d\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}(x,y)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda}-\nu_{t}^{\lambda,n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi^{\lambda,n}_{t}(x,y)d\bigl[\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr](x,y)dt

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\lambda,n}(x,y)d\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}(x,y)dt.

On the one hand, we can easily show from Lemma
A.9
and (
7.17
) that, for any
\lambda\in[-1,1]\setminus\{0\}
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda}-\nu_{t}^{\lambda,n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi^{\lambda,n}_{t}(x,y)d\bigl[\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr](x,y)dt=0.

On the other hand, using (
7.17
) again we get, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

(7.18)

\begin{split}&\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\lambda,n}(x,y)d\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}(x,y)dt\\
&\hskip 5.0pt\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}+|\lambda|\,\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}}\Bigl(\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{p}+|y|^{p})d\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}(x,y)\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\times\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{A}(1+|a|)d|\eta_{t}|(a)dt.\end{split}

and we conclude by Lemma
A.10
that the first
bound for

\|\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p})^{*}}

in
(
7.12
) holds.

Step 3.
We now establish the time regularity estimate in (
7.12
). We take
\lambda\in[-1,1]\setminus\{0\}
and
t_{1}<t_{2}\in[t_{0},T]
. Let
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p}
. Then, using the equation (
7.15
) for
\bm{\delta\gamma}^{\lambda}
we get

(7.19)

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\bigl[\delta\gamma^{\lambda}_{t_{2}}-\delta\gamma_{t_{1}}^{\lambda}\bigr](x,y)&=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda})\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi(x,y)d\bigl[\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr](x,y)dt\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi(x,y)d\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}(x,y).\end{split}

Thanks to (
7.12
) and the growth assumptions on
b
and
\nabla_{x}b
, the first term in the right-hand side of
(
7.19
)
can be bounded by

(7.20)

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda})\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi(x,y)d\bigl[\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr](x,y)dt\biggr|\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\leq C\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p}}\sup_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\bigl\|\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p})^{*}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\nu_{t}^{\lambda}|(a)dt.\end{split}

We then observe that

\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\nu_{t}^{\lambda}|(a)dt\leq C\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}\Bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}+\lambda\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\Bigr),

for some constant
C>0
independent of
t_{1}
,
t_{2}
and
\lambda
. Similarly, the second term in
(
7.19
)
can be bounded by

(7.21)

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\phi(x,y)d\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}(x,y)\biggr|\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\leq C\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}}\Bigl(\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})^{p/2}d\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}(x,y)\Bigr)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|)d|\eta_{t}|(a)dt.\end{split}

Once again, Lemma
A.10
gives

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})^{p/2}d\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}(x,y)\leq\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\lambda\bm{\eta},\gamma_{0}},

with

\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\lambda\bm{\eta},\gamma_{0}}

being as in the statement, see (
7.14
). Moreover, by definition of the norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}
, we have

\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|)d|\eta_{t}|(a)dt\leq\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}.

By inserting the last two displays in
(
7.21
)
and then
plugging
(
7.20
)
and
(
7.21
)
into
(
7.19
),
We easily deduce that (
7.12
) holds, which completes the third step of the proof.

Step 4.
In order to prove the last estimate (
7.13
) in the statement, we argue once again by duality. For
\lambda_{1}\neq\lambda_{2}\in[-1,1]\setminus\{0\}
, we let

\xi^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}_{t}:=[\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}]/(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})
, and easily verify that the latter satisfies

\partial_{t}\xi_{t}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}+\operatorname{div}_{x}\Bigl(b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{2}})\xi_{t}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}\Bigr)=-\operatorname{div}_{x}\Bigl(b(x,\eta_{t})\bigl[\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}+\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}\bigr]\Bigr),\quad t\in[t_{0},T],

with
\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}:=[\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}]/(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}).

The above equation has a structure very similar to that of
(
7.15
), with
\nu_{t}^{\lambda}
being replaced by

\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}
and
\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}
by

\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}+\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}
.

In order to repeat the duality argument used in the second step of
the proof, one must first study the regularity
properties of

\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}
and
\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}
.
By the first part
of (
7.12
), we know that

\delta\gamma^{\lambda_{2}}_{t}
is bounded in
(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p})^{*}
, independently of

t\in[t_{0},T]
and

\lambda_{2}\in[-1,1]\setminus\{0\}
. In fact, we claim that

\delta\gamma^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}_{t}
is also bounded in
(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p})^{*}
independently of

t\in[t_{0},T]
and

\lambda_{1}\neq\lambda_{2}\in[-1,1]\setminus\{0\}
.
This can also be seen as a consequence
of the first part of
(
7.12
), when
the parameter
\lambda

is shifted by

\lambda_{1}
: this amounts to replacing

\nu_{t}
by
\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}

and

(\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}-\gamma_{t}^{0})/\lambda

by

(\gamma_{t}^{\lambda+\lambda_{1}}-\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1}})/\lambda
, with
the latter being equal to

\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}

when
\lambda

is taken as
\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}
.

The next step is to examine what
(
7.18
)
becomes
when

\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}
is replaced by

\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}+\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}
.
Since the latter is now regarded as an element of the dual space

(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p})^{*}
, and no longer as an element of

{\mathcal{P}}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}})
,
this forces us to work with a test function

\phi

in

\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p}

(and not only in

\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}
).
The conclusion follows in the same way as the derivation of (
7.18
).

∎

We use the previous result to get the existence of a solution to the linearized equation
(
7.1
):

Proposition 7.7
.

Take
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
, for
p\in[2,3]
, and
\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}
in
\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
. Then,
the functions
(t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda})_{\lambda\in[-1,1]\setminus\{0\}}

converge, as
\lambda\rightarrow 0
and in
the space
\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*})
, toward some
\bm{\rho}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*})
, solution to the linearized equation (
7.1
). In particular, we have

\frac{d}{d\lambda}\Big|_{\lambda=0}\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}=\bm{\rho},\quad\mbox{ in }\ \mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*}\bigr),

which means that

\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\frac{\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}-\gamma_{t}}{\lambda}-\rho_{t}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*}}=0.

Moreover,
\bm{\rho}
satisfies the estimate

(7.22)

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p},\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}}\leq 1}|\langle\phi,\rho_{t}\rangle|+\sup_{t_{1}\neq t_{2}\in[t_{0},T]}\frac{\left\|\rho_{t_{2}}-\rho_{t_{1}}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*}}}{\sqrt{|t_{2}-t_{1}|}}\leq\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\gamma_{0}}\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})},

for a non-decreasing function
\Lambda:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
, independent of
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})
and
\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}
, and
where, for simplicity,
we used
\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\gamma_{0}}
as a short-hand notation:

\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\gamma_{0}}:=\Lambda\biggl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})^{p/2}d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\biggr).

Proof.

With the notation and estimates of the previous proposition we know that
[-1,1]\ \setminus\{0\}\ni\lambda\mapsto\bm{\delta\gamma}^{\lambda}=(\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda})_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*})
is Lipschitz continuous and therefore, by completeness of
\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*})
, extends uniquely at
\lambda=0
. We then find
\bm{\rho}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*})
such that

\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\bm{\delta\gamma}^{\lambda}=\bm{\rho},\quad\mbox{ in }\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*}).

Using the estimate
(
7.12
)
in the statement of Lemma
7.6

(assuming without any loss of generality that the function
\Lambda

in the statement of
Lemma
7.6

is right-continuous), we deduce that, for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p}
with
\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}}\leq 1
we have

|\langle\varphi;\rho_{t}\rangle|=\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}|\langle\varphi;\delta\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}\rangle|\leq\liminf_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\left\|\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p})^{*}}\leq\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\gamma_{0}}\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{{\mathcal{D}}(t_{0})},

and similarly, for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p}
with
\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p}}\leq 1
and any
t_{2}>t_{1}
in
[t_{0},T]
, we have

|\langle\varphi;\rho_{t_{2}}-\rho_{t_{1}}\rangle|=\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}|\langle\varphi;\delta\gamma^{\lambda}_{t_{2}}-\delta\gamma^{\lambda}_{t_{1}}\rangle|\leq\liminf_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\left\|\delta\gamma_{t_{2}}^{\lambda}-\delta\gamma_{t_{1}}^{\lambda}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*}}\leq\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\gamma_{0}}\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{{\mathcal{D}}(t_{0})}\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}},

where we used the same notation

\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\gamma_{0}}
as in the statement. We easily deduce that
\bm{\rho}
belongs to
\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p})^{*})
and
\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{p},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}}\leq 1}\langle\varphi;\rho_{t}\rangle
is bounded independently from
t\in[t_{0},T]
and
\bm{\rho}
satisfies estimate (
7.22
).

Let us now check that
\bm{\rho}
solves the linearized equation. For
\lambda\in[-1,1]\setminus\{0\}
,
\bm{\delta\gamma}^{\lambda}
solves (
7.15
).
In particular, for all test function
\varphi
satisfying the conditions (
2.27
)-(
2.28
), we deduce from
(
7.15
) that, for all
t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]
,

(7.23)

\langle\varphi_{t_{1}};\delta\gamma^{\lambda}_{t_{1}}\rangle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\langle\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}+b(\cdot,\nu^{\lambda}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\delta\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}\rangle dt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}(x,y)dt.

Thanks to the convergence of
\bm{\delta\gamma}
toward
\bm{\rho}
, and because
p\geq 2
,
we have the following two limits

\begin{split}&\limsup_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}|\langle\varphi_{t_{1}};\delta\gamma_{t_{1}}^{\lambda}\rangle-\langle\varphi_{t_{1}};\rho_{t_{1}}\rangle|\leq\left\|\varphi_{t_{1}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\bigl\|\delta\gamma_{t_{1}}^{\lambda}-\rho_{t_{1}}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}=0,\\
&\limsup_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}|\langle\partial_{t}\varphi_{t};\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}-\rho_{t}\rangle|\leq\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\bigl\|\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}-\rho_{t}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}=0.\end{split}

Moreover, for all
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\Bigl|\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{\lambda})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr\rangle-\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\bigr\rangle\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq|\lambda|\,\Bigl|\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr\rangle\Bigr|\,+\,\Bigl|\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}-\rho_{t}\bigr\rangle\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq|\lambda|\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\bigl\|\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})^{*}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\eta_{t}|(a)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+C\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\bigl\|\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}-\rho_{t}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{3})d|\eta_{t}|(a),

for a constant
C
independent of
\lambda
and
t
. Using the fact that
\bm{\eta}\in{\mathcal{D}}(t_{0})

and letting
\lambda\rightarrow 0
in (
7.23
),
we deduce that
\bm{\rho}
is solution to the linearized equation (
7.1
).
∎

7.1.3.
Uniqueness

The next lemma will be useful to prove uniqueness of solutions
to the linearized continuity equation (
7.1
). It also explains why we require
\bm{\rho}
to be bounded in
(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3})^{*}
although the equation makes sense if
\bm{\rho}
is only bounded in
(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})^{*}
. Notice that, to obtain existence of solutions that are bounded in
(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3})^{*}
, we need
\gamma_{0}
to be in
\mathcal{P}_{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
and not only
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
.

Lemma 7.8
.

Take
\rho\in(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}
such that

\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq 1}|\langle\varphi;\rho\rangle|<+\infty.

Then,

(7.24)

\Bigl\{\forall\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2,1}\quad\langle\phi;\rho\rangle=0\Bigr\}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\rho=0\quad\mbox{ in }(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}.

Proof.

For all
R>1
, we consider a smooth cut-off function

\chi_{R}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow[0,1]
satisfying

\chi_{R}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1\quad\mbox{\rm in}&B(0,R)\\
0\quad\mbox{\rm outside}&B(0,R+1)\end{array}\right.,

with

\left\|\chi_{R}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}}\leq c
, for some
c>0
independent from
R
.
We easily check that there is
C>0
, depending on
c
, such that, for all
R>1
and
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}
,

\bigl\|\phi(1-\chi_{R})\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq\frac{C}{R}\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}.

Now, we consider
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}
together with a sequence of functions

(\phi^{n})_{n\geq 1}
in
\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2,1}
, bounded in
\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}
and converging to
\phi
in
\mathcal{C}^{2}_{\rm loc}
. Then, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
R>1
, we rewrite

\phi
in the form

(7.25)

\phi=\phi^{n}+(\phi-\phi^{n})\chi_{R}+(\phi-\phi^{n})(1-\chi_{R}).

We now take the duality bracket with respect to

\rho\in(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}
. Assuming
that
\rho
satisfies the condition in the left-hand side of (
7.24
), we observe that
\langle\phi^{n};\rho\rangle=0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, because each
\phi^{n}
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2,1}
. Next, we estimate the duality bracket between
\rho

and the second and third terms in the right-hand side of
(
7.25
). We have

\begin{split}&\bigl|\bigl\langle(\phi-\phi^{n})\chi_{R};\rho\bigr\rangle\bigr|\leq C\bigl\|\phi-\phi^{n}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(B(0,R+1))},\\
&\bigl|\bigl\langle(\phi-\phi^{n})(1-\chi_{R});\rho\bigr\rangle\bigr|\leq C\bigl\|(\phi-\phi^{n})(1-\chi_{R})\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq CR^{-1}\bigl(\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}+\left\|\phi^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\bigr).\end{split}

We easily deduce, by letting first
n\rightarrow+\infty
and then
R\rightarrow+\infty
in
(
7.25
),
that
\langle\phi;\rho\rangle=0.

∎

As announced, we use Lemma

7.8
to prove
the following uniqueness result:

Proposition 7.9
.

Let
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
,
\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
, and
\bm{\gamma}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}))
.
Then, the linearized equation
(
7.1
), understood as in
(
2.27
)–(
2.28
)–(
2.30
),
has at most one solution
\bm{\rho}
satisfying, in addition to
(
2.29
),
the condition

(7.26)

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq 1}|\langle\varphi;\rho_{t}\rangle|<+\infty.

Proof.

Let
\bm{\rho}^{1},\bm{\rho}^{2}
be two solutions to
(
7.1
),
satisfying both
(
2.29
)
and (
7.26
). Let also

(\bm{\nu}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
be the regularization of
\bm{\nu}
from Lemma
A.9
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
t_{1}\in(t_{0},T]
and
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2,1}
, we call
\bm{\varphi}^{n}
the solution to (
A.17
) with control
\bm{\nu}^{n}
. Then, by Lemma
A.6
, see also Remark
A.9
,
\bm{\varphi}^{n}
is an admissible test function for the equations for
\bm{\rho}^{1}
and
\bm{\rho}^{2}
. Then, we have

\bigl\langle\phi;\rho^{2}_{t_{1}}-\rho^{1}_{t_{1}}\bigr\rangle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\bigl\langle[b(\cdot,\nu_{t})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})]\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n};\rho_{t}^{2}-\rho_{t}^{1}\bigr\rangle dt.

For every
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
it holds

\displaystyle\bigl|\bigl\langle[b(\cdot,\nu_{t})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})]\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n};\rho_{t}^{2}-\rho_{t}^{1}\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})]\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\left\|\rho_{t}^{2}-\rho_{t}^{1}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}\left\|\rho_{t}^{2}-\rho_{t}^{1}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})^{*}}.

By Proposition
2.6
and Lemma
A.9
we have

\sup_{n\geq 1}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}<+\infty

and, by Lemma
A.9
again we also have

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}dt=0.

By combining the last four displays, we deduce that
\langle\phi;\rho_{t_{1}}^{2}-\rho_{t_{1}}^{1}\rangle=0
for all
t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]
and all
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2,1}
. We conclude using Lemma
7.8
.
∎

Combined with Proposition
7.7
this gives the first part of Proposition
2.19
. It remains to prove the representation formula (
2.33
).

7.1.4.
Representation formula

Notice that, for any
\lambda_{0}\in(-1,1)
, we can apply the result above replacing
\bm{\nu}
by
\bm{\nu}+\lambda_{0}\bm{\eta}
and deduce that
\lambda\mapsto\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}
is differentiable over
(-1,1)
,
the derivative being given, for all
\lambda\in(-1,1)
, by

(7.27)

\frac{d}{d\lambda}\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}=\bm{\rho}^{\lambda}\quad\mbox{ in }\ \mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}),

where
\bm{\rho}^{\lambda}
is the unique solution in
\mathcal{R}(t_{0})
to

(7.28)

\partial_{t}\rho_{t}^{\lambda}+\operatorname{div}_{x}(b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda})\rho_{t}^{\lambda})=-\operatorname{div}_{x}(b(x,\eta_{t})\gamma_{t}^{\lambda})\quad\mbox{ in }(t_{0},T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\rho_{t_{0}}^{\lambda}=0.

Notice that, with this notation, the solution
\bm{\rho}
of (
7.1
) is simply
\bm{\rho}^{0}
.
We conclude with a useful expression for
\bm{\rho}^{\lambda}
.When
\lambda=0
, it gives an explicit formula for
\bm{\rho}
.

Proposition 7.10
.

Take
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
and
\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
. For
\lambda\in(-1,1)
, let
\bm{\rho}^{\lambda}
be the unique solution to (
7.28
), as given by
Proposition
2.19
. Then, for all
t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]
,
\rho^{\lambda}_{t_{1}}
is given, for all
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2}
by

(7.29)

\langle\phi;\rho^{\lambda}_{t_{1}}\rangle=\lambda\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr\rangle dt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}(x,y)dt,

where
\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}=(\gamma^{\lambda}_{t})_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}
is the solution to the continuity equation (
7.11
) starting from
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})
and controlled by
\bm{\nu}+\lambda\bm{\eta}
, and
\varphi:[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is the solution to the transport equation

-\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}-b(x,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}=0\quad\mbox{ in }(t_{0},t_{1})\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\varphi_{t_{1}}=\phi\quad\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we use the same notation as in the statement. For all
\lambda\in(-1,1)
we have, using the equations for
\bm{\varphi}
and
\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}
and applying Lemma
A.12
,

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\gamma_{t_{1}}^{\lambda}(x,y)&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\varphi_{t_{0}}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\lambda\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt.\end{split}

The strategy is to differentiate both sides of the equality with respect to
\lambda
. To do so, we observe that, for
h\in(-1,1)
such that
\lambda+h
belongs to
(-1,1)
, it holds

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\frac{\gamma_{t}^{\lambda+h}-\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}}{h}-\rho_{t}^{\lambda}\Bigr\rangle dt\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\biggl\|\frac{\gamma_{t}^{\lambda+h}-\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}}{h}-\rho_{t}^{\lambda}\biggr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\left\|b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}dt.

By assumptions on
b
(see
Assumption (
Regularity
)
) and Proposition
A.11
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\left\|b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{3})d|\eta_{t}|(a)dt

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla_{x}\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{3})d|\eta_{t}|(a)dt<+\infty.

Back to the penultimate display, we deduce from
the identity

[d/d\lambda]|_{\lambda=0}\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}=\bm{\rho}

in
\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*})
(see (
2.31
)) that

\frac{d}{d\lambda}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}(x,y)dt=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}^{\lambda}\rangle dt.

The rest follows easily.
∎

We can use this explicit representation to extend
\bm{\rho}
to test functions with only one spacial derivative.

Proposition 7.11
.

Take
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
and
\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
, and let
\bm{\rho}
be the only solution in
\mathcal{R}(t_{0})
to (
7.1
). Then, for all
t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]
,
\rho_{t_{1}}
extends uniquely to
\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}
and it is given, for any
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}
by

\langle\phi;\rho_{t_{1}}\rangle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}(x,y)dt,

where
\varphi:[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is the solution to

(7.30)

-\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}-b(x,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}=0\quad\mbox{ in }[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\varphi_{t_{1}}=\phi\quad\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}.

In particular, for all
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}
and all
t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]
, it holds

(7.31)

\langle\phi;\rho_{t_{1}}\rangle=\frac{d}{d\lambda}\Big|_{\lambda=0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\gamma_{t_{1}}^{\lambda}(x,y)=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}(x,y)dt.

Proof.

Proposition

A.11

makes it possible to define a continuous linear form on

(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})^{*}
, by letting, for every
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}
,

\langle\phi;\widetilde{\rho_{t_{1}}}\rangle:=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}(x,y)dt.

Thanks to
Lemma

A.10
and because

\gamma_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{3}({\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{3}})
, we then check that

\sup_{\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2},\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq 1}|\langle\phi;\widetilde{\rho_{t_{1}}}\rangle|<+\infty.

Thanks to Proposition
7.10
(with
\lambda=0
),
\widetilde{\rho_{t_{1}}}
coincides with
\rho_{t_{1}}
over
\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2}
. We then proceed similarly to Lemma
7.8
to prove that the extension is unique.
It remains to justify that the expression for the derivative (
7.31
) is valid for any function
\phi\in\mathcal{C}_{2}^{1}
. To this end, we use Lemma
A.12
to write, for
\lambda\in(-1,1)\setminus\{0\}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\bigl[\frac{\gamma_{t_{1}}^{\lambda}-\gamma_{t_{1}}}{\lambda}\bigr](x,y)=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt.

Therefore, it suffices to prove the continuity at
\lambda=0
of

\lambda\mapsto\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt.

First, we easily justify from the ODE representation that
\lambda\mapsto\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}
is continuous in
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
for all
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
. By Proposition
A.11
,
(x,y)\mapsto b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)
is continuous with at most quadratic growth for Lebesgue almost all
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
and therefore, at
these times,

\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}(x,y).

Moreover, by
Lemma
A.10

and
Proposition
A.11
, there is a constant
C>0
independent of
\lambda
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\lambda\in(-1,1)}\biggl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt\leq C\left\|\nabla_{x}\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\bigl(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\bigr)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\int_{A}(1+|a|)d|\eta_{t}|(a).

Since the right-hand side belongs to
L^{1}([t_{0},t_{1}])
, we conclude by Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem that

\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}(x,y)dt,

which completes the proof.
∎

7.1.5.
Stability

We continue with some further results on the application
\lambda\in[-1,1]\mapsto\bm{\rho}^{\lambda}
where
\bm{\rho}^{\lambda}
is the unique solution
of the equation
(
7.28
)
in the space
\mathcal{R}(t_{0})
.

First, we have the following regularity estimate with respect to
\lambda
.

Lemma 7.12
.

There exists a non-decreasing function
\Lambda:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
such that, for

(t_{0},\gamma_{0})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
and
\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
,

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\Bigl\|\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}\Bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}\leq\Lambda_{\gamma_{0},\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}}|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|,\quad\mbox{ for all }\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in(-1,1),

where
\Lambda_{\gamma_{0},\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}}
is a short-hand notation for

\Lambda_{\gamma_{0},\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}}=\Lambda\biggl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}+\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})^{3/2}d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\biggr).

Proof.

We argue by duality again. We first notice that, by making the difference in the equations for
\bm{\rho}^{\lambda_{2}}
and
\bm{\rho}^{\lambda_{1}}
(recall again (
7.28
)), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{1}})+\operatorname{div}_{x}(b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{1}})(\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}))=

\displaystyle-(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\operatorname{div}_{x}(b(x,\eta_{t})\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}})

\displaystyle-\operatorname{div}_{x}(b(x,\eta_{t})(\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}))\quad\mbox{ in }(t_{0},T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}.

We fix
t_{1}\in(t_{0},T]
and
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}
and we let
\bm{\varphi}^{n}:[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be the solution to the backward equation (
7.30
) with control
\bm{\nu}^{\lambda_{1},n}
–the already used regularization of
\bm{\nu}^{\lambda_{1}}
,
see for instance (
7.16
) together with the first step in the proof of Lemma

7.6
– with terminal condition
\phi
at
t_{1}
. Using the equation satisfied by the difference
\bm{\rho}^{\lambda_{2}}-\bm{\rho}^{\lambda_{1}}

and
the equation satisfied by
\bm{\varphi}^{n}
, we have

(7.32)

\begin{split}\langle\phi;\rho_{t_{1}}^{\lambda_{2}}-\rho_{t_{1}}^{\lambda_{1}}\rangle&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}-\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{1},n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n};\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}\rangle dt\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1})\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n};\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}\rangle dt\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}(x,y)d(\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1}})(x,y)dt\\
&=:I^{n}_{1}+I^{n}_{2}+I^{n}_{3}.\end{split}

We start with
I_{1}^{n}
:

\displaystyle|I_{1}^{n}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\bigl\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}-\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{1},n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\bigl\|\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3})^{*}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\bigl\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}-\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{1},n})\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\bigl\|\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3})^{*}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\bigl\|\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3})^{*}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\bigl\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}-\nu_{t}^{\lambda_{1},n})\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}dt

and we use Proposition
A.11
and Lemma
A.9
to conclude that
\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}I_{1}^{n}=0
. We then proceed with
I_{2}^{n}
:

\displaystyle|I_{2}^{n}|

\displaystyle\leq|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\left\|b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\bigl\|\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3})^{*}}dt

\displaystyle\leq|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\bigl\|\rho_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3})^{*}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\eta_{t}|(a)dt

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta},\gamma_{0}}|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|,

for a function
\Lambda
as in the statement.
Then, we use Lemma
7.6
to handle
I_{3}^{n}
:

\displaystyle|I_{3}^{n}|

\displaystyle\leq|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\biggl\|\frac{\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{2}}-\gamma_{t}^{\lambda_{1}}}{\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}}\biggr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})^{*}}\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\eta_{t}|(a)dt

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda_{\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta},\gamma_{0}}|\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}|.

Inserting the last three displays in (
7.32
) and letting

n
tend to
+\infty
, we complete the proof.
∎

We can go one step further and establish the following
weak differentiability property of
the function

\lambda\in[-1,1]\mapsto\bm{\rho}^{\lambda}
, where
we recall again that
\bm{\rho}^{\lambda}
is the unique solution
of the equation
(
7.28
)
in the space
\mathcal{R}(t_{0})
:

Lemma 7.13
.

For all
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2,1}
,
\lambda\mapsto\langle\phi;\rho_{T}^{\lambda}\rangle
is differentiable at
0
and we have

\frac{d^{2}}{d\lambda^{2}}\Big|_{\lambda=0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\gamma_{T}^{\lambda}(x,y)=\frac{d}{d\lambda}\Big|_{\lambda=0}\langle\phi;\rho_{T}^{\lambda}\rangle=2\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\bigr\rangle dt,

where
\varphi:[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is the solution to

-\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}-b(x,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}=0\quad\mbox{ in }[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\varphi_{T}=\phi\quad\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}.

Proof of Lemma
7.13
.

We make
use of the expression
(
7.29
), for two different values of
the parameter therein:
\lambda\not=0
and
0
.
Making the difference between these two expressions
and dividing the result by
\lambda
, we get:

(7.33)

\langle\phi;\delta\rho_{T}^{\lambda}\rangle=\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr\rangle dt+\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt,

where we used the notations
\delta\rho_{T}^{\lambda}:=\lambda^{-1}(\rho_{T}^{\lambda}-\rho_{T})
and
\delta\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}:=\lambda^{-1}(\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}-\gamma_{t})
,
recalling that
\bm{\rho}
and
\bm{\gamma}

are shortened notations for

\bm{\rho}^{0}
and
\bm{\gamma}^{0}
.

It remains to pass to the limit in the right-hand side when
\lambda\rightarrow 0
. For the first term in the right-hand side of
(
7.33
), we obtain

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr\rangle dt-\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\bigr\rangle dt\biggr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}^{\lambda}-\rho_{t}\bigr\rangle dt\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\bigl\|\rho_{t}^{\lambda}-\rho_{t}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{3})d|\eta_{t}|(a)dt.

We then use Proposition
A.11
and Lemma
7.12
to deduce that the right-hand side goes to
0
with
\lambda
, which concludes the analysis of the first term in the right-hand side of (
7.33
).

We handle the second term in a similar manner, but using Proposition
7.7
instead of Lemma
7.12
. As a consequence, we get

\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0}\langle\phi;\delta\rho_{T}^{\lambda}\rangle=2\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\bigl\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\bigr\rangle dt,

which is te desired result.

∎

7.2.
The Linearized Transport Equation

We now turn to the linearized transport equation

(7.34)

-\partial_{t}v_{t}-b(x,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t}=b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}\quad\mbox{ in }[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad v_{T}=0\quad\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},

where
u
is the solution to

(7.35)

-\partial_{t}u_{t}-b(x,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}=0\quad\mbox{ in }[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad u_{T}=L.

We take
\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
.
Denoting the right-hand side
of (
7.34
) by

f_{t}(x,y)=b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x,y)
, we deduce
from the regularity assumptions on
b
(see
Assumption (
Regularity
)
) and Proposition
A.11
that

\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|f_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}}dt+\sup_{t_{1}<t_{2}\in[t_{0},T]}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\left\|f_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}dt\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}+\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr).

The above bound provides an indication on the space within which the equation
(
7.34
)
should be solved. Precisely,
we say that
v\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1})
is a solution if, for all
t_{1}<t_{2}\in[t_{0},T]
and all
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
it holds

(7.36)

v_{t_{1}}(x,y)-v_{t_{2}}(x,y)=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\bigl\{b(x,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t}(x,y)+b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x,y)\bigr\}dt.

Similarly to Proposition
A.11
, we have the following result (the proof is identical and thus omitted):

Proposition 7.14
.

There is a unique solution to the linearized transport equation
(
7.34
). For all
t\in[t_{0},T]
,
v_{t}
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{2}
and we have the estimate

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}}+\sup_{t_{1}<t_{2}\in[t_{0},T]}\frac{\left\|v_{t_{2}}-v_{t_{1}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}}{\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})},

for a non-decreasing function

\Lambda:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, independent of
t_{0}
and

{\bm{\nu}}
.

Moreover, for all
t\in[t_{0},T]
and all
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
, the solution is given by

(7.37)

v_{t}(x,y)=\int_{t}^{T}b\bigl(X_{s}^{t,x},\eta_{s})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{s}\bigl(X_{s}^{t,x},y\bigr)ds,

where
(X_{s}^{t,x})_{s\geq t}
is the flow of the ODE, solution to

\dot{X}^{t,x}_{s}=b(X_{s}^{t,x},\nu_{s})\quad s\geq t,\quad X_{t}^{t,x}=x.

When
\bm{\nu}
and
\bm{\eta}
satisfy some further regularity we can improve the regularity of the solution. In this case, the equation is satisfied in the classical sense.

Proposition 7.15
.

Assume that
t\mapsto\nu_{t}
and
t\mapsto\eta_{t}
are continuous from
[t_{0},T]
to
\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{3}}(A)
. Then,
v

(as given by (
7.37
))
and its gradient
\nabla_{x}v
are jointly continuously differentiable in
(t,x,y)
.

Proof.

We only briefly sketch the argument for
\nabla_{x}v
. Using
Proposition
A.1
(see also Remark
A.2
) and Lemma
A.6
, we can differentiate twice in space the representation formula (
7.37
)
and then deduce that the functions

(t,x,y)\mapsto\nabla v_{t}
and
(t,x,y)\mapsto\nabla^{2}v_{t}
are jointly continuous.
By differentiating once in the
x
-variable the equation (
7.34
) for
v_{t}
we get
\partial_{t}\nabla_{x}v_{t}=\nabla_{x}\bigl\{-b(x,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t}-b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}\bigr\}
and deduce from the time continuity
of

t\mapsto\nu_{t}
and
t\mapsto\eta_{t}
, as assumed in the statement, that
\partial_{t}\nabla_{x}v_{t}
is also jointly continuous.
∎

As expected, the solution
v

to
(
7.34
), as provided by Proposition

7.14
, can be retrieved by linearizing the transport equation
(
7.35
)
with respect to the control parameter.

Lemma 7.16
.

Take
t_{0}\in[0,T]
and
\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
. Let
\bm{u}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1})
be the solution to the backward equation

-\partial_{t}u_{t}-b(x,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}=0\quad\mbox{ in }[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad u_{T}=L\quad\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}.

For all
\lambda\in(-1,1)
, with
\lambda\neq 0
, let
\bm{\nu}^{\lambda}:=\bm{\nu}+\lambda\bm{\eta}
and
\bm{u}^{\lambda}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1})
be the solution to the backward equation

-\partial_{t}u^{\lambda}_{t}-b(x,\nu^{\lambda}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{\lambda}_{t}=0\quad\mbox{ in }[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad u^{\lambda}_{T}=L\quad\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}.

Letting
\delta\bm{u}^{\lambda}:=\lambda^{-1}(\bm{u}^{\lambda}-\bm{u})
, for
\lambda\not=0
, we have the estimate

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\bigl\|\delta u^{\lambda}_{t}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}}+\sup_{t_{1}<t_{2}\in[t_{0},T]}\frac{\|\delta u^{\lambda}_{t_{2}}-\delta u^{\lambda}_{t_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}}{\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})},\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr),

for some non-decreasing function
\Lambda:\mathbb{R}_{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}
independent
of
\lambda\in(-1,1)
and
t_{0}\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

For
t\in[t_{0},T]
and
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
, we let
(X^{\lambda,t,x}_{s})_{s\in[t,T]}
be the solution to

\dot{X}^{\lambda,t,x}_{s}=b(X^{\lambda,t,x}_{s},\nu^{\lambda}_{s}),\quad s\in[t,T];\quad X^{\lambda,t,x}_{t}=x.

Applying (
A.29
) in Lemma
A.12
with
t_{0}=t
,
t_{1}=T
,
\phi=L
,
\bm{\nu}^{1}=\bm{\nu}^{\lambda}
,
\bm{\nu}^{2}=\bm{\nu}
and
\gamma_{0}=\delta_{(x,y)}
, and therefore with
(\gamma^{1}_{s})_{s\in[t_{0},T]}:=(\delta_{(X_{s}^{\lambda,t,x},y)})_{s\in[t_{0},T]}
therein, we get

\delta u_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)=\int_{t}^{T}b(X_{s}^{\lambda,t,x},\eta_{s})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{s}(X_{s}^{\lambda,t,x},y)ds.

We easily deduce from Proposition
A.1
and Proposition
A.11
that

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\bigl\|\delta u_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}+\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr).

By making the difference between the equation for
\bm{u}^{\lambda}
and the equation for
\bm{u}
and then dividing by
\lambda
, we also find that
\delta\bm{u}^{\lambda}
solves

-\partial_{t}\delta u_{t}^{\lambda}-b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda})\cdot\nabla_{x}\delta u_{t}^{\lambda}=-b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}\quad\mbox{ in }[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad v_{T}(x,y)=0.

Integrating in time and recalling
(
2.2
)
in Definition
2.4
, we find

\displaystyle\left\|\delta u_{t_{2}}-\delta u_{t_{1}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\left\{b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{\lambda})\cdot\nabla_{x}\delta u_{t}^{\lambda}+b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}\right\}dt\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}+\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\Bigl(\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\delta u^{\lambda}_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}+\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}u_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}\Bigr)\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}},

and we conclude using the previous estimate together with Proposition
A.11
.
∎

We conclude with a useful duality relation.

Proposition 7.17
.

Take
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
and
\bm{\nu},\bm{\eta}^{1},\bm{\eta}^{2}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
. Let
(\bm{\gamma},\bm{u})
be the solutions to the continuity and transport equations
(
2.8
)
and
(
7.35
) with control
\bm{\nu}
, and
(\bm{\rho}^{i},\bm{v}^{i})
be the solutions to the linearized equations
(
7.1
)
and
(
7.34
)
associated to
\bm{\eta}^{i}
for
i=1,2
.
Then, we have

\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,\eta^{2}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t};\rho^{1}_{t}\Bigr\rangle dt=\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta^{1}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}v^{2}_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}(x,y)dt.

Proof.

For
\lambda\in(-1,1)
, we let
\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}
be the solution to the continuity equation
(
2.8
)
when driven by the control
\bm{\nu}^{\lambda}:=\bm{\nu}+\lambda\bm{\eta}^{1}.
We use Lemma

A.9

in order to
regularize
\bm{\nu}
and
\bm{\eta}^{2}
,
with

(\bm{\nu}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
and
(\bm{\eta}^{2,n})_{n\geq 1}
as regularized sequences.
Then, for each
n\geq 1
, we
call
\bm{u}^{n}

the solution to the transport equation (
7.35
)
driven by
{\bm{\nu}}^{n}
.
Also, we
let
\bm{v}^{2,n}
be the classical solution to
the equation
(
7.34
)
associated to
the input
({\bm{\nu}}^{n},\bm{\eta}^{2,n},\bm{u}^{n})
, i.e.

-\partial_{t}v_{t}^{2,n}-b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t}^{2,n}=b(x,\eta_{t}^{2,n})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{n},\quad v_{T}^{2,n}=0,

with the regularity of

\bm{v}^{2,n}
being given by
Proposition
7.15
.

Using the equations satisfied by
\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}
first and
\bm{v}^{2,n}
next, we
can follow the derivation of
(
A.30
)
and then obtain (noticing that
v_{T}^{2,n}
is null)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}v_{t_{0}}^{2,n}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)

\displaystyle=-\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\bigl\{\partial_{t}v_{t}^{2,n}(x,y)+b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda})\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t}^{2,n}(x,y)\bigr\}d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt

(7.38)

\displaystyle=-\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda}-\nu_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t}^{2,n}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t}^{2,n})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{n}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt.

The objective now is to pass to the limit as

n\rightarrow+\infty
.
Denoting,
for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
,
(X_{t}^{n,x})_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
the solution to the ODE
(
A.1
)
starting from
x
at time
t_{0}
and driven by the control
{\bm{\nu}}^{n}
(we remove the superscript

n
when
\bm{\nu}^{n}
is replaced by
\bm{\nu}
),
we know from Corollary

A.5
that, for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\left\|X_{s}^{n,\cdot}-X_{s}^{\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{1}}=0.

By the representation formula in Proposition

A.11
, we deduce that

(x,y)\mapsto(\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{n}(x,y),\nabla^{2}_{x}u_{t}^{n}(x,y))
converges locally uniformly to

(x,y)\mapsto(\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x,y),\nabla_{x}^{2}u_{t}(x,y))
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
.

Moreover, we know from Lemma

A.9
that
that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\Bigl(\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{1}}+\left\|b(\cdot,\eta_{t}^{n})-b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{1}}\Bigr)dt=0.

By
using formula (
7.37
) (with
X^{t,x}_{s}
being replaced by
X^{n}_{s}
,

\eta_{s}
by
\eta_{s}^{2,n}
and
\nabla_{x}u_{s}
by
\nabla_{x}u^{n}_{s}
)
to represent
v^{2,n}
, we deduce from the last three convergence properties that, for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
,

(x,y)\mapsto v^{2,n}_{t}(x,y)
converges
to

(x,y)\mapsto v^{2}_{t}(x,y)

locally uniformly.
And then, by using the first inequality in the statement
of Proposition

7.14
,
we can let
n\rightarrow+\infty
in
(
7.38
) to infer that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}v^{2}_{t_{0}}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)

\displaystyle=-\lambda\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta^{1}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}v^{2}_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt

\displaystyle+\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta^{2}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt.

In particular, when
\lambda=0
we get

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}v^{2}_{t_{0}}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)=\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta^{2}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{0}(x,y)dt.

Substracting the two previous equalities and dividing by
\lambda
gives

\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t}^{2})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x,y)d\bigl[\frac{\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}-\gamma_{t}^{0}}{\lambda}\bigr](x,y)dt=\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta^{1}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}v^{2}_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}(x,y)dt.

Thanks to Proposition

7.7
, we can pass to the limit when
\lambda\rightarrow 0
in the left-hand side, while estimate (
7.12
) in Lemma
7.6
together with the regularity of
\bm{v}^{2}
from Proposition
7.14
allows us to pass to the limit in the right-hand side.
∎

8.
Second Order Conditions

The purpose of this section is to prove the main results of Subsection

2.4
, and in particular Theorem

2.39

and
Proposition

2.24
.

8.1.
Second Order Variations

We start with following statement, which corresponds to the first part of Theorem

2.39
(recall Definition
2.17

for the definition of the set
\mathcal{A}^{\ell}(t_{1})
used below):

Proposition 8.1
.

Let
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{3}({\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}})
and

\bm{\nu}^{*}
be minimum of
J((t_{0},\gamma_{0}),\cdot)
with associated optimal trajectory
\bm{\gamma}^{*}
and
t_{1}\in[t_{0},T)
. Then,
\mathcal{J}((t_{1},\gamma^{*}_{t_{1}}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta})\geq 0

for all
\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{A}^{\ell}(t_{1})
.

Proof.

Step 1.

We first assume that
\eta\in\mathcal{A}^{\ell}(t_{1})
also satisfies

(8.1)

\bm{\eta}\mbox{ belongs to }L^{\infty}([t_{1},T]\times A)\mbox{ with }\bm{\eta}=0\mbox{ outside }[t_{1},T]\times B(0,R)\mbox{ for some }R>1.

Thanks to the lower bound on
\bm{\nu}^{*}
from Proposition
2.9
,
\bm{\nu}^{\lambda}:=\bm{\nu}^{*}+\lambda\bm{\eta}
belongs
to
\mathcal{A}(t_{1})
for all
\lambda\in(-\lambda_{0},\lambda_{0})
, for some
\lambda_{0}\in(0,1)
small enough
(recall Definition
2.1
for the definition of

{\mathcal{A}}(t_{1})
). We denote by
\bm{\gamma}^{\lambda}=(\gamma^{\lambda}_{t})_{t\in[t_{1},T]}
the resulting curve, solution to

\partial_{t}\gamma^{\lambda}_{t}+\operatorname{div}_{x}\bigl(b(x,\nu_{t}^{\lambda})\gamma_{t}^{\lambda}\bigr)=0,\quad\mbox{ in }(t_{1},T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\gamma_{t_{1}}^{\lambda}=\gamma^{*}_{t_{1}}.

By dynamic programming,
\bm{\nu}^{*}
(once restricted to
[t_{1},T]
) is optimal for
J((t_{1},\gamma^{*}_{t_{1}}),\cdot)
and therefore,

(8.2)

J\bigl((t_{1},\gamma^{*}_{t_{1}}),\bm{\nu}^{\lambda}\bigr)\geq J\bigl((t_{1},\gamma^{*}_{t_{1}}),\bm{\nu}^{*}\bigr),\quad\forall\lambda\in(-\lambda_{0},\lambda_{0}).

We are going to compute the second order variation of
\lambda\in(-\lambda_{0},\lambda_{0})\mapsto J((t_{1},\gamma^{*}_{t_{1}}),\bm{\nu}^{\lambda})
.
The terminal cost is handled by Lemma
7.13
(which relies on the notation introduced in
(
7.27
), with the interval
(-1,1)
being replaced by

(-\lambda_{0},\lambda_{0})
).
It holds

\frac{d^{2}}{d\lambda^{2}}\Big|_{\lambda=0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}L(x,y)d\gamma_{T}^{\lambda}(x,y)=2\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{*}(x,y)dt,

where
{\bm{u}}^{*}
solves the
transport equation
in (
2.12
).

On the other hand, using Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem together with condition (
8.1
) and Proposition
2.9
(which supplies us with the proper integrability conditions), we have

\frac{d}{d\lambda}\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\mathcal{E}(\nu_{t}^{\lambda}|\nu^{\infty})dt=\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\ell(a)\eta_{t}(a)dadt+\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\log(\nu_{t}^{\lambda}(a))d\eta_{t}(a)dt

and,

(8.3)

\frac{d^{2}}{d\lambda^{2}}\Big|_{\lambda=0}\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\mathcal{E}\bigl(\nu_{t}^{\lambda}|\nu^{\infty}\bigr)dt=\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\frac{\eta_{t}(a)^{2}}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}dadt.

Using (
8.2
)
(and recalling the expression
(
1.2
) for
the cost
J

and the formula
(
2.4
)
for
{\mathcal{J}}
), we deduce from the last two displays that, for all
\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{A}^{\ell}(t_{1})
satisfying the condition (
8.1
) it holds

(8.4)

\mathcal{J}\bigl((t_{1},\gamma_{t_{1}}^{*}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta}\bigr)=\frac{d^{2}}{d\lambda^{2}}\Big|_{\lambda=0}J\bigl((t_{1},\gamma^{*}_{t_{1}}),\bm{\nu}^{\lambda}\bigr)\geq 0.

Step 2.

It remains to drop Condition (
8.1
)
in order to get
the result for any
{\bm{\eta}}\in{\mathcal{A}}^{\ell}(t_{1})
. We proceed by
an approximation argument. For that we take

\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{A}^{\ell}(t_{1})
with associated curve
\bm{\rho}\in\mathcal{R}(t_{1})
solution to (
2.26
)
(with
\bm{\nu}
being understood as
\bm{\nu}^{*}
therein). We can assume that
\eta_{t}
has a density for all
t\in[t_{1},T]
and

(8.5)

\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\frac{\eta_{t}(a)^{2}}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}dadt<+\infty,

as otherwise
\mathcal{J}\bigl(t_{1},\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta}\bigl)=+\infty
(see
(
2.4
)) and
(
8.4
)
trivially holds true. For
R>0
, we
consider the cutoff function
\varphi_{R}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
:

(8.6)

\varphi_{R}(r):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}r&\mbox{ if }|r|\leq R,\\
0&\mbox{ otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

By the lower bound
(
2.17
)
for
\bm{\nu}^{*}
, we can find
R_{0}>0
such that, for all
R\geq R_{0}
and

t\in[t_{0},T]
, it holds
\int_{B(0,R)}\nu^{*}_{t}(a^{\prime})da^{\prime}\geq 1/2
. Then, we define the following approximation

\bm{\eta}^{R}
of
\bm{\eta}
, by letting

\eta_{t}^{R}(a):=\varphi_{R}\bigl(\eta_{t}(a)\bigr)\mathds{1}_{B(0,R)}(a)-\frac{\int_{B(0,R)}\varphi_{R}(\eta_{t}(a^{\prime}))da^{\prime}}{\int_{B(0,R)}\nu^{*}_{t}(a^{\prime})da^{\prime}}\nu^{*}_{t}(a)\mathds{1}_{B(0,R)}(a),\quad(t,a)\in[t_{1},T]\times A,

where
B(0,R)
of the open ball of center
0
and radius

R
in
A
. We easily verify that
\bm{\eta}^{R}
belongs to
\mathcal{A}^{\ell}(t_{1})
for all
R\geq R_{0}
, satisfies the condition (
8.1
) and converges point-wise to
\bm{\eta}
when
R\rightarrow+\infty
(because
\bm{\eta}
is centered). Moreover,
we have, for all
(t,a)\in[t_{1},T]\times A
,

(8.7)

\bigl|\eta_{t}^{R}(a)\bigr|\leq|\eta_{t}(a)|+2\biggl(\int_{A}|\eta_{t}(a^{\prime})|da^{\prime}\biggr)\nu^{*}_{t}(a).

By Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem,
we deduce that
\bm{\eta}^{R}
converges to
\bm{\eta}
in
\mathcal{D}(t_{1})
equipped with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{1})}

defined in
(
2.4
) (since

\bm{\eta}
and

\bm{\nu}^{*}
themselves belong to

{\mathcal{D}}(t_{1})
). Thanks to (
8.5
) (and again to
the domination property (
8.7
)), we can also conclude that

(8.8)

\lim_{R\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\frac{|\eta_{t}^{R}(a)|^{2}}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}dadt=\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\frac{|\eta_{t}(a)|^{2}}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}dadt.

On the other hand, if we let
\bm{\rho}^{R}
be the solution to (
2.26
) associated to
\bm{\eta}^{R}
, we can apply the estimate
(
2.32
)
of Proposition
2.19
(after noticing that
\bm{\rho}^{R}-\bm{\rho}
is the solution associated to
\bm{\eta}^{R}-\bm{\eta}
) to find
C>0
independent from
R>0
such that (recall
that
\gamma_{t_{1}}^{*}
belongs to

{\mathcal{P}}_{3}({\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}})

because
\gamma_{0}
does)

\left\|\bm{\rho}^{R}-\bm{\rho}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{1})}\leq C\left\|\bm{\eta}^{R}-\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{1})},

and then both sides tend to
0
as
R
tends to
\infty
. Using (
2.16
), we deduce

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t}^{R})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*};\rho_{t}^{R}\rangle dt-\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*};\rho_{t}\rangle dt\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt\leq\biggl|\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t}^{R}-\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*};\rho_{t}^{R}\rangle dt\biggr|+\biggl|\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*};\rho_{t}^{R}-\rho_{t}\rangle dt\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt\leq C\left\|\bm{\rho}^{R}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{1})}\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\eta_{t}^{R}-\eta_{t}|(a)dt+C\left\|\bm{\rho}^{R}-\bm{\rho}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{1})}\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\eta_{t}|(a)dt

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt\leq C\Bigl(\left\|\bm{\eta}^{R}-\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{1})}+\left\|\bm{\rho}^{R}-\bm{\rho}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{1})}\Bigr).

We deduce that

\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\langle b(\cdot,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle dt=\lim_{R\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\langle b(\cdot,\eta^{R}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t};\rho^{R}_{t}\rangle dt.

Combining the above display with (
8.8
)
and recalling once
again
the formula
(
2.4
)
for
{\mathcal{J}}
,
we obtain

\mathcal{J}\bigl((t_{1},\gamma^{*}_{t_{1}}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta}\bigr)=\lim_{R\rightarrow+\infty}\mathcal{J}\bigl((t_{1},\gamma^{*}_{t_{1}}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta}^{R}\bigr)\geq 0,

with the inequality in the right-hand side following from
the first step. This concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

8.2.
Necessary and Sufficient Conditions

We now establish the second part of Theorem

2.39
.

Proposition 8.2
.

Given

(t_{0},\gamma_{0})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
,
let
\bm{\nu}^{*}
be a minimum of
J((t_{0},\gamma_{0}),\cdot)
with associated optimal trajectory and multiplier
(\bm{\gamma}^{*},\bm{u}^{*})
. Then,
for

t_{1}\in[t_{0},T)

and for
\bm{\eta}\in\mathcal{A}^{\ell}(t_{1})
with associated solution
\bm{\rho}\in\mathcal{R}(t_{1})
to the advection equation (
2.26
),
\mathcal{J}((t_{1},\gamma^{*}_{t_{1}}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta})=0
if and only if there exists
\bm{v}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{1},T],\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1})
such that
(\bm{\eta},\bm{\rho},\bm{v})
solves (
2.38
)-(
2.39
).

Proof.

Step 1.
In this step, we prove that the
conditions are sufficient. To do so, we first assume that there exists
\bm{v}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{1},T],\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1})
such that
(\bm{\nu},\bm{\rho},\bm{v})
solves (
2.38
) and (
2.39
).
Using the equation
(
2.38
) satisfied by
\bm{\eta}
and recalling that, for all
t\in[t_{1},T]
,
\eta_{t}(A)=0
, we have, for
dt
-almost all
t\in[t_{1},T]
,

\begin{split}&\int_{A}\frac{(\eta_{t}(a))^{2}}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}da=\int_{A}\frac{\eta_{t}(a)}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}d\eta_{t}(a)\\
&=-\frac{1}{\epsilon}\int_{A}\biggl(\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\rho_{t}\Bigr\rangle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t}(x,y)d\gamma^{*}_{t}(x,y)\biggr)d\eta_{t}(a).\end{split}

Integrating in time and combining the above display with Proposition
7.17
and the formula (
2.4
),
we get

\mathcal{J}\bigl((t_{1},\gamma_{t_{1}}^{*}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta}\bigr)=0.

Since Proposition
8.1
shows that the left-hand side is always non-negative,
\bm{\eta}
is indeed a minimum of
\mathcal{J}((t_{1},\gamma_{t_{1}}^{*}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\cdot)
.

Step 2.
We now prove that the conditions are necessary. Let us consider a minimum

\widetilde{\bm{\eta}}=(\widetilde{\eta}_{t})_{t_{1}\leq t\leq T}
of

\mathcal{J}((t_{1},\gamma_{t_{1}}^{*}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\cdot)
in
(
2.4
)
in

{\mathcal{A}}^{\ell}(t_{1})

with associated curve

\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}=(\widetilde{\rho}_{t})_{t_{1}\leq t\leq T}

in
{\mathcal{R}}(t_{1})
. We also let

\widetilde{\bm{v}}=(\widetilde{v}_{t})_{t_{1}\leq t\leq T}
be the solution to

(8.9)

-\partial_{t}\widetilde{v}_{t}-b(x,\nu^{*}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\widetilde{v}_{t}=b(x,\widetilde{\eta}_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t}\mbox{ in }(t_{1},T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\widetilde{v}_{T}=0.

Take another admissible couple

(\bm{\eta},\bm{\rho})\in{\mathcal{A}}^{\ell}(t_{1})\times{\mathcal{R}}(t_{1})
with finite cost
\mathcal{J}((t_{1},\gamma_{t_{1}}^{*}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta})
. For
\lambda\in(-1,1)
we let
(\bm{\eta}^{\lambda},\bm{\rho}^{\lambda}):=(1-\lambda)(\widetilde{\bm{\eta}},\widetilde{\bm{\rho}})+\lambda(\bm{\eta},\bm{\rho})
which is also admissible. Noticing that

\lambda\mapsto\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\rho_{t}^{\lambda}\Bigr\rangle d\eta_{t}^{\lambda}(a)dt

is polynomial (of order two) in
\lambda
, we easily compute

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\lambda}\Big|_{\lambda=0}\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\rho_{t}^{\lambda}\Bigr\rangle d\eta_{t}^{\lambda}(a)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\rho_{t}-\widetilde{\rho}_{t}\Bigr\rangle d\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a)dt

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\widetilde{\rho}_{t}\Bigr\rangle d(\eta_{t}-\widetilde{\eta}_{t})(a)dt.

In order to handle
the first term in the right-hand side, we use the equations satisfied by
\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}
and
\widetilde{\bm{v}}
together with Proposition
7.17
with

(\bm{\rho}^{1},\bm{\eta}^{1})=(\bm{\rho}-\widetilde{\bm{\rho}},\bm{\eta}-\widetilde{\bm{\eta}})
and

(\bm{\rho}^{2},\bm{\eta}^{2})=(\widetilde{\bm{\rho}},\bm{\widetilde{\eta}})
therein
and Proposition
7.1
, to get

(8.10)

\begin{split}&\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\rho_{t}-\widetilde{\rho}_{t}\Bigr\rangle d\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a)dt\\
&=\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\widetilde{v}_{t}(x,y)d\gamma^{*}_{t}(x,y)d(\eta_{t}-\widetilde{\eta}_{t})(a)dt.\end{split}

This implies

(8.11)

\begin{split}&\frac{d}{d\lambda}\Big|_{\lambda=0}\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\rho_{t}^{\lambda}\Bigr\rangle d\eta_{t}^{\lambda}(a)dt\\
&=\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\Bigl(\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\widetilde{\rho}_{t}\Bigr\rangle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\widetilde{v}_{t}(x,y)d\gamma^{*}_{t}(x,y)\Bigr)d(\eta_{t}-\widetilde{\eta}_{t})(a)dt,\end{split}

which permits to handle the derivative w.r.t.

\lambda
of the second term in the definition of the cost

\mathcal{J}((t_{1},\gamma_{t_{1}}^{*}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta}^{\lambda})

in (
2.4
).

Now, we deal with the first part of the cost
\mathcal{J}((t_{1},\gamma_{t_{1}}^{*}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta}^{\lambda})

in (
2.4
). Since
\mathcal{J}((t_{1},\gamma_{t_{1}}^{*}),\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\eta})
is finite,
\bm{\eta}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure on
[t_{1},T]\times A
with a density (still written)
(t,a)\mapsto\eta(t,a)
in
L^{2}((\bm{\nu}^{*})^{-1})
. This allows us to differentiate the quadratic map

\lambda\mapsto\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\frac{(\eta_{t}^{\lambda})^{2}(a)}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}dadt,

and find

(8.12)

\frac{d}{d\lambda}\Big|_{\lambda=0}\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\frac{(\eta_{t}^{\lambda})^{2}(a)}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}dadt=2\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\frac{\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a)(\eta_{t}(a)-\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a))}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}dadt.

By minimality of
\widetilde{\bm{\eta}}
we deduce from
(
8.11
)
and
(
8.12
) that, for all admissible
\bm{\eta}
with finite cost,

\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\Bigl(l_{t}(a)+\frac{\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a)}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}\Bigr)d(\eta_{t}-\widetilde{\eta}_{t})(a)dt=0,

where the function
l
is defined by

(8.13)
