l_{t}(a):=\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\widetilde{\rho}_{t}\Bigr\rangle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\widetilde{v}_{t}(x,y)d\gamma^{*}_{t}(x,y),\quad(t,a)\in[t_{1},T]\times A.

Taking
\bm{\eta}
in the form

\eta_{t}(a)=\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a)+\xi_{t}(a)-\int_{A}\xi_{t}(a^{\prime})da^{\prime},\quad(t,a)\in[t_{1},T]\times A,

for an arbitrary smooth function
\xi:[t_{1},T]\times A\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with compact support, we get (thanks to Fubini’s theorem)

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\biggl[l_{t}(a)+\frac{\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a)}{\nu_{t}^{*}(a)}-\int_{A}\biggl(l_{t}(a^{\prime})+\frac{\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a^{\prime})}{\nu_{t}^{*}(a^{\prime})}\biggr)da^{\prime}\biggr]\xi_{t}(a)dadt

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{T}\int_{A}\Bigl(l_{t}(a)+\frac{\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a)}{\nu_{t}^{*}(a)}\Bigr)\biggl(\xi_{t}(a)-\int_{A}\xi_{t}(a^{\prime})da^{\prime}\biggr)dadt=0,

and we deduce that

(8.14)

l_{t}(a)+\frac{\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a)}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a)}=\int_{A}\Bigl(l_{t}(a^{\prime})+\frac{\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a^{\prime})}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a^{\prime})}\Bigr)da^{\prime},

for Lebesgue almost every
(t,a)\in[t_{1},T]\times A
. Recalling that for
dt
-almost every
t\in[t_{1},T]
,
\eta_{t}^{*}(A)=0
and
\nu^{*}_{t}(A)=1
, we deduce from (
8.14
) (after multiplying both sides by
\nu^{*}_{t}(a)
and integrating in
a
) that

\int_{A}l_{t}(a)d\nu^{*}_{t}(a)=\int_{A}\Bigl(l_{t}(a^{\prime})+\frac{\widetilde{\eta}_{t}(a^{\prime})}{\nu^{*}_{t}(a^{\prime})}\Bigr)da^{\prime},\quad\mbox{for Lebesgue almost every }t\in[t_{1},T].

Getting back to (
8.14
) and recalling the definition
(
8.13
)
of
l
, we get the result.
∎

8.3.
Additional Regularity

Similarly to the first order conditions we can prove some more regularity for solutions to the linearized system.

Proof of Proposition
2.24
.

By Proposition
2.9
we know that
t\mapsto\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{3}}(A)
is continuous. Thanks to Proposition
7.15
, it therefore suffices to prove that
t\mapsto\eta_{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{3}}(A)
is continuous. From the exponential integrability of
\bm{\nu}
given by Proposition
2.9
, the regularity of
\nabla_{x}u
from (
2.16
) in Proposition
2.9
, the facts that
\bm{\rho}
belongs to
\mathcal{R}(t_{1})
(recall Definition
2.18
) and the fact that
{\bm{v}}
belongs to
\mathcal{C}([t_{1},T],\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1})
by Proposition
2.21
, we first deduce from the explicit formula (
2.38
) for
\bm{\eta}
that

(8.15)

\sup_{t\in[t_{1},T]}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{m})d|\eta_{t}|(a)<+\infty,

for any
m\geq 1
. Using the representation formula (
2.37
) for
\bm{v}
we easily deduce that
\sup_{t\in[t_{1},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1}}
is finite. Using the time regularity of
t\mapsto\nu_{t}
from Proposition
2.9
and Lemma
7.4
we deduce that
t\mapsto(a\mapsto\nu_{t}(a)\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle)\in\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{3}}(A)
is continuous over
[t_{1},T]
. Now for
t_{3}\geq t_{2}\in[t_{1},T]
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t_{3}}(x,y)d\gamma_{t_{3}}(x,y)-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t_{2}}(x,y)d\gamma_{t_{2}}(x,y)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}(v_{t_{3}}-v_{t_{2}})d\gamma_{t_{3}}(x,y)\Big|+\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t_{2}}(x,y)d(\gamma_{t_{3}}-\gamma_{t_{2}})(x,y)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(1+|a|)\left\|\nabla_{x}v_{t_{3}}-\nabla_{x}v_{t_{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})^{3/2}d\gamma_{t_{3}}(x,y)

\displaystyle+C(1+|a|^{2})\left\|\nabla_{x}v_{t_{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1}}\left\|\gamma_{t_{3}}-\gamma_{t_{2}}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1})^{*}}

and we conclude from Proposition
2.21
, Proposition
2.4
and Lemma
6.5
together with the regularity of
t\mapsto\nu_{t}
that
t\mapsto(a\mapsto\nu_{t}(a)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}(x,y))
is continuous from
[t_{1},T]
to
\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{3}}(A)
. The continuity of
t\mapsto c_{t}
the normalizing constant is handled similarly.
∎

9.
Proofs of Section
3

In this section we prove the various results presented in Section
3
. We make repeated use of the objects and notation introduced therein.

We start with

Proof of Proposition
3.5
.

Step 1. Convergence of
({\bm{\eta}}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

For each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
we consider
{\bm{\eta}}^{n}
as a finite signed measure on
[t_{0}^{n},T]\times A
, which we can decompose (with standard notations) into

{\bm{\eta}}^{n}={\bm{\eta}}^{n,+}-{\bm{\eta}}^{n,-}
.
Without any difficulty,

{\bm{\eta}}^{n,\pm}
can be extended in a trivial manner to
(the Borel subsets of)

[0,T]\times A
, by letting for any Borel subset
B
of
[0,T]\times A
,

{\bm{\eta}}^{n,\pm}(B)={\bm{\eta}}^{n,\pm}(B\cap([t_{0}^{n},T]\times A))
.
We can extend
{\bm{\eta}}^{n}
to
[0,T]\times A
in a similar manner while preserving the decomposition

{\bm{\eta}}^{n}={\bm{\eta}}^{n,+}-{\bm{\eta}}^{n,-}
.
From Lemma
3.3
, we have

(9.1)

\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A}\bigl(1+|a|^{4}\bigr)d{\bm{\eta}}^{n,\pm}(t,a)<\infty.

And then, we deduce that, up to a subsequence,
the two sequences of positive measures
({\bm{\eta}}^{n,+})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}

and

({\bm{\eta}}^{n,-})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}

converge weakly to some positive measures
{\bm{\eta}}^{+}
and

{\bm{\eta}}^{-}
(on
[0,T]\times A
).
It is standard to see that

(9.2)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A}\bigl(1+|a|^{4}\bigr)d{\bm{\eta}}^{\pm}(t,a)\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A}\bigl(1+|a|^{4}\bigr)d{\bm{\eta}}^{n,\pm}(t,a)\leq C.

And, by a uniform integrability argument, it holds, for all continuous
function
f\in\mathcal{C}([0,T]\times A)
satisfying

|f_{t}(a)|\leq C(1+|a|^{3+\delta})

for some
(C,\delta)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times(0,1)
,

(9.3)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A}f_{t}(a)d{\bm{\eta}}^{n,\pm}(t,a)=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{A}f_{t}(a)d{\bm{\eta}}^{\pm}(t,a).

It remains to see that the time marginal laws
of
{\bm{\eta}}^{+}
and
{\bm{\eta}}^{-}

are absolutely continuous (with respect to the Lebesgue measure
on
[0,T]
). Repeating the second part of the proof of Lemma
2.13
word for word,
we deduce that there exists a constant
C
such that for any

n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and any Borel subset
I
of
[0,T]
,

(9.4)

\int_{I\times A}d{\bm{\eta}}^{n,\pm}(t,a)\leq C\sqrt{|I|},

where
|I|
denotes the Lebesgue measure of
I
. By weak convergence, (
9.4
) still holds at the limit for
\bm{\eta}^{+}
and
\bm{\eta}^{-}
. In particular we can find
two positive functions
g^{+}
and
g^{-}
in
L^{1}([0,T])
and two collections of
regular conditional probability distributions
(n_{t}^{+}(\cdot))_{t\in[0,T]}

and

(n_{t}^{-}(\cdot))_{t\in[0,T]}
on
A
such that, for any bounded
Borel function
f:[0,T]\times A\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
,

\int_{[0,T]\times A}f(t,a)d{\bm{\eta}}^{\pm}(t,a)=\int_{0}^{T}\biggl(\int_{A}f_{t}(a)dn_{t}^{\pm}(a)\biggr)g_{t}^{\pm}dt.

Letting
\eta_{t}^{\pm}:=g_{t}^{\pm}\cdot n_{t}^{\pm}
, we deduce that

\int_{[0,T]\times A}f(t,a)d{\bm{\eta}}^{\pm}(t,a)=\int_{0}^{T}\biggl(\int_{A}f_{t}(a)d\eta_{t}^{\pm}(a)\biggr)dt,

which is the required identity. In particular, letting
\eta_{t}:=\eta_{t}^{+}-\eta_{t}^{-}
, for any

t\in[0,T]
, we recover the fact that
d{\bm{\eta}}(t,a)

can be disintegrated as
d\eta_{t}(a)dt
.
Choosing

f_{t}(a)
in (
9.3
) as a function independent of
a

and using the fact that
\eta^{n}_{t}(A)=0
for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}

and
t\in[0,T]
, we get that
\eta_{t}(A)=0
for (almost every)
t\in[0,T]
.

Also, choosing
f_{t}(a)
to be zero for
t>t_{0}-\epsilon
for
\epsilon>0
arbitrarily small and using the fact that
\eta^{\pm,n}_{t}=0
for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
(almost every)
t\in[0,t_{0})
, we deduce from (
9.3
) that
\eta^{\pm}_{t}=0
for
t<t_{0}
.

Now we take
t_{1}<t_{2}\in(t_{0},T]
and
f
a bounded, continuous function over
[t_{1},t_{2}]\times A
. Approximating the function
\mathbf{1}_{[t_{1},t_{2}]}
by continuous functions and using the uniform bound (
9.4
) we can show that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}f_{t}(a)d\eta^{n}_{t}(a)dt=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}f_{t}(a)d\eta_{t}(a)dt.

By a uniform integrability argument, using the uniform bound (
9.1
) we show that this limit holds when
f
has growth of order
(1+|a|^{3+\delta})
for
\delta\in(0,1)
. In particular, for all
t_{2}>t_{1}
in
(t_{0},T]
we have

\frac{1}{\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d\eta^{\pm}_{t}(a)dt=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d\eta^{n,\pm}_{t}(a)dt<+\infty.

Combined with (
9.2
) this shows that
\bm{\eta}
belongs to
\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
and

\left\|\bm{\eta}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left\|\bm{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0}^{n})}.

Step 2. Convergence of
({\bm{v}}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Following

Step 1
, we first extend

{\bm{v}}^{n}
, for each

n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
to
[0,T]
by letting

v^{n}_{t}=v^{n}_{t_{0}^{n}}

for
t\in[0,t_{0}^{n})
. This does not change the estimate from Lemma
3.3
.
Then, the combination of the two bounds for
\bm{v}^{n}
in (
3.3
)
shows that
the functions

(v^{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and

(\nabla_{x}v^{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}

are uniformly bounded and
uniformly
continuous
on
[0,T]\times B
for any closed ball
B
of
\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
. It follows by Ascoli’s theorem that there is
v\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1})
such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0}\vee t_{0}^{n},T]}\left\|v_{t}^{n}-v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(B)}=0.

In particular, we easily deduce that

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|v^{n}_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}.

Now we can consider functions
(\chi_{R})_{R>0}
such that

\chi_{R}(x,y)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\mbox{ if }(x,y)\in B(0,R)\\
0&\mbox{ if }(x,y)\notin B(0,R+1)\end{array}\right.

and
\left\|\chi_{R}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{b}}\leq c
for some
c>0
independent from
R
, where
B(0,R)
is the ball of
\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
of radius
R
, centered at the origin. Then, for every
R>0
and every
n\geq 1
it holds

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}\left\|v_{t}^{n}-v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}\left\|\chi_{R}(v_{t}^{n}-v_{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}+\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}\left\|(1-\chi_{R})(v_{t}^{n}-v_{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}\left\|v_{t}^{n}-v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(B(0,R+1))}+\frac{C}{R}(\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n},T]}\left\|v_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}+\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}).

The claim follows by taking first
R\rightarrow+\infty
and then
n\rightarrow+\infty
.

Step 3. Convergence of
({\bm{\rho}}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Following
Step 2
, we extend
{\bm{\rho}}^{n}
, for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, to
[0,T]
by letting

{\rho}_{t}^{n}={\rho}^{n}_{t_{0}^{n}}
for

t\in[0,t_{0}^{n})
. Then, thanks to the uniform estimate on
\left\|\bm{\rho}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{0}^{n})}
from Lemma
3.3
we can apply the compactness result of Lemma
A.14
to find
{\bm{\rho}}\in\mathcal{R}(t_{0})
such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}=0.

Step 4. Proof of
(
3.13
).
It remains to show
that
({\bm{\rho}},{\bm{v}})

solves (
3.13
). Obviously, the strategy is to pass to the limit in
(
3.10
).

By combining Lemma
2.15

and the uniform bound on
\left\|\bm{\eta}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0}^{n})}
from Lemma
3.3
, we first observe that

(9.5)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}}^{T}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{4})|\nu_{t}^{n}(a)-\nu^{*}_{t}(a)|dadt=0.

Following the proof of Lemma
7.16
we can rewrite, for all
t\in(t_{0},T]
and all
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
and all
n
large enough such that
t_{0}^{n}<t
,

(9.6)

v_{t}^{n}(x,y)=\int_{t}^{T}b(X_{s}^{n,t,x},\eta_{s}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*,n}_{s}(X_{s}^{n,t,x},y)ds

where
(X_{s}^{n,t,x})_{s\in[t,T]}
is the solution to

\dot{X}_{s}^{n,t,x}=b(X_{s}^{n,t,x},\nu_{s}^{n}),\quad s\in[t,T];\quad X_{t}^{n,t,x}=x.

Similarly we can introduce the solution
(X_{s}^{*,t,x})_{s\in[t,T]}
to the ODE

\dot{X}_{s}^{*,t,x}=b(X_{s}^{,x},\nu^{*}_{s}),\quad s\in[t,T];\quad X_{t}^{*,t,x}=x.

Using the regularity properties of

b
together with
(
9.5
), we get

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{s\in[t,T]}|X_{s}^{n,t,x}-X_{s}^{*,t,x}|=0.

Recall that, by item
iii)
in Property
(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
,
(\bm{\nu}^{*,n},\bm{\gamma}^{*,n},\bm{u}^{*,n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is assumed to converge to
(\bm{\nu}^{*},\bm{\gamma}^{*},\bm{u}^{*})
in the sense of Lemma
2.15
. Thanks to the convergence of
(\bm{\eta}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
from
Step 1

it is then quite easy to pass to the limit in
(
9.6
)
and to get
the formula

v_{t}(x,y)=\int_{t}^{T}b(X_{s}^{t,x},\eta_{s})\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{s}(X_{s}^{t,x},y)ds,\quad(t,x,y)\in(t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}.

By a continuity argument, the representation formula also holds at
t=t_{0}
. The first equation in
(
3.13
)
follows.

We now address the second equation in (
3.13
).
To prove that
it is satisfied, we
fix
t_{1}\in(t_{0},T]
and return to the second equation
in (
3.10
)(for
n
sufficiently large so that
t_{0}^{n}<t_{1}
). We take a test function
\varphi:[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfying (
2.27
)- (
2.28
) and some time
t_{2}\in(t_{1},T]
. Given the weak formulation of the equation we have

\langle\varphi_{t_{2}};\rho_{t_{2}}^{n}\rangle=\langle\varphi_{t_{1}};\rho_{t_{1}}^{n}\rangle+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\langle\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}+b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}^{n}\rangle dt+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{*,n}(x,y)dt.

Using the regularity of
\varphi,\partial_{t}\varphi
and
\nabla_{x}\varphi
, together with the regularity of
b
and the convergence of
(\bm{\rho}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
to
\bm{\rho}
from Step
iii)
of the proof we first obtain that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\Bigl(\langle\varphi_{t_{2}};\rho_{t_{2}}^{n}\rangle,\langle\varphi_{t_{1}};\rho_{t_{1}}^{n}\rangle,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\langle\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}+b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{*})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}^{n}\rangle dt\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\langle\varphi_{t_{2}};\rho_{t_{2}}\rangle,\langle\varphi_{t_{1}};\rho_{t_{1}}\rangle,\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\langle\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}+b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{*})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle dt\Bigr).

Now we observe that
(\bm{\rho}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
being bounded in
\mathcal{R}(t_{0})
there is
C_{\varphi}>0
independent from
n
such that

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\langle b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n}-\nu_{t}^{*})\cdot

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}^{n}\rangle dt\Bigr|\leq C_{\varphi}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n}-\nu_{t}^{*})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{b}}dt\leq C_{\varphi}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\nu_{t}^{n}-\nu_{t}^{*}|(a)dt

(9.7)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varphi}\lambda_{n}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\eta_{t}^{n}|(a)dt+C_{\varphi}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\nu_{t}^{*,n}-\nu_{t}^{*}|(a)dt

The first term in the second line is handle by the boundness of
(\bm{\eta}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
and the fact that
\lambda_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
. The second term is handled item
iii)
in
Property
(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
. Combined with (
9.7
) this shows that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\langle b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}^{n}\rangle dt=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\langle b(x,\nu_{t}^{*})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle dt.

Now we handle the term involving
\bm{\gamma}^{*,n}
that we decompose into

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}

\displaystyle b(x,\eta_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{*,n}(x,y)dt-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{*}(x,y)dt

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d(\gamma_{t}^{*,n}-\gamma_{t}^{*})(x,y)dt

(9.8)

\displaystyle+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t}^{n}-\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{*}(x,y)dt

We estimate the first term by

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d(\gamma_{t}^{*,n}-\gamma_{t}^{*})(x,y)dt\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\left\|\gamma_{t}^{*,n}-\gamma_{t}^{*}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1})^{*}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\left\|b(\cdot,\eta_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C_{\varphi}\sup_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\left\|\gamma_{t}^{*,n}-\gamma_{t}^{*}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1})^{*}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\eta^{n}_{t}|(a)dt.

By item
iii)
in
Property
(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
in and Lemma
6.5

\sup_{t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]}\left\|\gamma_{t}^{*,n}-\gamma_{t}^{*}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1})^{*}}
goes to
0
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
while
\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\eta_{t}^{n}|(a)dt
is bounded independently from
n
by Lemma
3.3
. For the last term in (
9.8
) we use Fubini’s theorem, see in particular Appendix
7.1.1
to rewrite it as

(9.9)

\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}\Bigl\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{*}(x,y)\Bigr\}d(\eta_{t}^{n}-\eta_{t})(a)dt

and observe that the integrand is continuous with linear growth in
a
, uniformly in
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
. This makes it possible to apply the conclusions of the first step of this proof to justify that (
9.9
) converges to
0
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
.
We can go back to (
9.8
) to infer that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{*,n}(x,y)dt=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{*}(x,y)dt

an conclude that

\langle\varphi_{t_{2}};\rho_{t_{2}}\rangle=\langle\varphi_{t_{1}};\rho_{t_{1}}\rangle+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\langle\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}+b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle dt+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\eta_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)d\gamma_{t}^{*}(x,y)dt

for all
t_{1},t_{2}\in(t_{0},T]
with
t_{1}<t_{2}
. By a continuity argument we then propagate this equality to
t_{1}=t_{0}
. It remains to notice that
\langle\varphi_{t_{0}};\rho_{t_{0}}\rangle=0
. But this follows from the uniform bound on
\left\|\bm{\rho}^{n}\right\|_{n\in\mathcal{R}(t_{0}^{n})}
since it gives

|\langle\varphi_{t_{0}};\rho_{t_{0}}\rangle|=|\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\langle\varphi_{t_{0}};\rho_{t_{0}}^{n}\rangle|\leq C_{\varphi}\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left\|\rho^{n}_{t_{0}}-\rho^{n}_{t_{0}^{n}}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}\leq C_{\varphi}\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\bigl\{\left\|\bm{\rho}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{0}^{n})}\sqrt{|t_{0}^{n}-t_{0}|}\bigr\}=0.

∎

Proof of Proposition
3.6
.

Step 1.
t\mapsto k_{t}\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}(A)
is bounded.
For all
t\in[t_{0},T]
we have

\left\|k_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}(A)}\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\cdot)\cdot\nabla_{x}v_{t}(x,y)d\gamma^{*}_{t}(x,y)\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}(A)}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left\|a\mapsto\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\rho_{t}\rangle\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}(A)}

For the first term, we use formula (
1.24
) with
q=1,k=0,p=3
, and for the second term, the same formula with
q=k=1
and
p=3
and we obtain

\left\|k_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}(A)}\leq C_{b}\bigl(\left\|\nabla_{x}v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})^{3/2}d\gamma_{t}^{*}(x,y)+\left\|\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3})^{*}}\bigr),

for some constant
C_{b}>0
depending only on
b
.

Step 2. Convergence of
k_{t}^{n}
to
k_{t}
in
\mathcal{C}^{0}_{3}(A)
.

Now, for
t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]
and
a\in A
we have, by assumptions on
b
,

(9.10)

\begin{split}|k_{t}^{n}(a)-k_{t}(a)|&=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\biggl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,a)\cdot d\Bigl\{\bigl(\nabla_{x}v_{t}^{n}-\nabla_{x}v_{t}\bigr)\gamma_{t}^{n}+\nabla_{x}v_{t}\bigl(\gamma_{t}^{n}-\gamma^{*}_{t}\bigr)\Bigr\}(x,y)\\
&\hskip 45.0pt+\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\bigl(\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*,n}-\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t}\bigr);\rho_{t}^{n}\Bigr\rangle+\Bigl\langle b(\cdot,a)\cdot\nabla_{x}u^{*}_{t};\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}\Bigr\rangle\biggr|\\
&\leq C(1+|a|)\left\|\nabla_{x}v_{t}^{n}-\nabla_{x}v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})d\gamma_{t}^{n}(x,y)\\
&+C(1+|a|^{2})\left\|\nabla_{x}v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1}}\left\|\gamma_{t}^{n}-\gamma_{t}^{*}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1})^{*}}\\
&+C(1+|a|^{2})\left\|\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*,n}-\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\left\|\rho_{t}^{n}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3})^{*}}+C(1+|a|^{3})\left\|\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\left\|\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}},\end{split}

for some
C>0
independent from
t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]
,
a\in A
and
n\geq 1
. Recalling the regularity estimates from Proposition
2.21
and Proposition
2.6
for
\bm{v}
and
\bm{u}^{*}
respectively as well as Lemma
2.4
for
\bm{\gamma}^{n}
and Proposition
3.5
for
\bm{\rho}^{n}
, we have the uniform bounds

\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}],T}\Bigl\{\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})d\gamma_{t}^{n}(x,y)+\left\|\nabla_{x}v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1}}+\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\rho_{t}^{n}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3})^{*}}+\left\|\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\Bigr\}<+\infty.

And then, after noticing that
\gamma_{t}^{n}-\gamma_{t}^{*}=\lambda_{n}\rho_{t}^{n}+\gamma_{t}^{*,n}-\gamma_{t}^{*}
we have, by item
ii)
of Property
(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
for the convergence of
(\bm{\gamma}^{*,n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
to
\bm{\gamma}^{*}
and
(\bm{u}^{*,n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
to
\bm{u}^{*}
and Proposition
3.5
for the convergence of
(\bm{v}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
to
\bm{v}
and
(\bm{\rho}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
to
\bm{\rho}
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}\Bigl\{\left\|\nabla_{x}v_{t}^{n}-\nabla_{x}v_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{2}}+\left\|\gamma_{t}^{n}-\gamma_{t}^{*}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2,1})^{*}}+\left\|\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*,n}-\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}+\left\|\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}\Bigr\}=0.

Combined together this leads to

(9.11)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}\left\|k_{t}^{n}-k_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{3}(A)}=0.

Given the integrability of
\nu_{t}^{*,n}
from Lemma
3.1
we easily deduce that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}\int_{A}|k_{t}^{n}(a)-k_{t}(a)|d\nu_{t}^{*,n}(a)=0.

Step 3. Higher-order convergence when the limit is
(0,0,0)
.

We now assume that
(\bm{\eta},\bm{\rho},\bm{v})=(0,0,0)
. In particular, this implies that
\bm{k}=0
.
Using the assumptions on
b
we can estimate
|\nabla_{a}k^{n}_{t}(a)|
as follows. First we use the regularity assumption on
b
to find
C>0
independent from
t\in[t_{0}^{n},T]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that

|\nabla_{a}k_{t}^{n}(a)|\leq C\Bigl((1+|a|)\left\|\nabla_{x}v_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})^{3/2}d\gamma_{t}^{n}(x,y)+(1+|a|^{3})\left\|\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{*,n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1}}\left\|\rho_{t}^{n}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}\Bigr).

The term involving
\bm{\gamma}^{n}
is bounded independently from
t\in[t_{0}^{n},T]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
thanks to Lemma
3.3
and Lemma
2.4
.
By Proposition
3.5
and recalling that we assume
(\bm{\rho},\bm{v})=(0,0)
we get,

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\Bigl\{\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}v_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{2}}+\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n},T]}\left\|\rho_{t}^{n}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}\Bigr\}=0.

Combined with (
9.11
) we get

(9.12)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}\left\|k_{t}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}(A)}=0.

It remains to prove that

(9.13)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}}^{T}\int_{A}|\nabla_{a}k_{t}^{n}(a)|^{2}d\nu_{t}^{n}(a)=0.

To this end we use the definition of
\lambda_{n}
to obtain

(9.14)

\int_{t_{0}^{n}}^{T}\int_{A}|\nabla_{a}k_{t}^{n}(a)|^{2}d\nu_{t}^{n}(a)dt=\int_{t_{0}^{n}}^{T}\int_{A}|\nabla_{a}k_{t}^{n}(a)|^{2}d\nu_{t}^{*,n}(a)dt+\lambda_{n}\int_{t_{0}^{n}}^{T}\int_{A}|\nabla_{a}k_{t}^{n}(a)|^{2}d\eta_{t}^{n}(a)dt

On the one hand, using the integrability of
\nu_{t}^{*,n}
from Lemma
3.1
and (
9.12
) we get

(9.15)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}}^{T}\int_{A}|\nabla_{a}k_{t}^{n}(a)|^{2}d\nu_{t}^{*,n}(a)dt=0.

On the other hand, using the bound on
\bm{k}^{n}
in
\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}(A)
from Lemma
3.3
we find
C>0
independent from
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\Bigl|\int_{t_{0}^{n}}^{T}\int_{A}|\nabla_{a}k_{t}^{n}(a)|^{2}d\eta_{t}^{n}(a)\Bigr|\leq C\int_{t_{0}^{n}}^{T}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{4})d|\eta_{t}^{n}|(a)dt,

and the right-hand side is bounded from above independently from
n\in\mathbb{N}
by Lemma
3.3
. Combined with (
9.15
) in (
9.14
) and recalling that
\lambda_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
this shows that (
9.13
) holds.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.7
.

The proof follows from Lemmas
9.17
,
9.2
and
9.3
below.

∎

Lemma 9.1
.

In the setting of Proposition
3.7
we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}}^{T}\Bigl(\int_{A}\Bigl|\frac{\Gamma_{t}[\bm{\nu}^{n}](a)-\nu_{t}^{*,n}(a)}{\lambda_{n}}

\displaystyle+\nu_{t}^{*}(a)\bigl(k_{t}(a)-\int_{A}k_{t}(a^{\prime})d\nu_{t}^{*}(a^{\prime})\bigr)\Bigr|da\Bigr)^{2}dt

(9.16)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}}^{T}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{6}\int_{A}|I_{t}^{i,n}(a)|da\Bigr)^{2}dt.

where
\bm{I}^{i,n}:=(I_{t}^{i,n})_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}
is defined, for
1\leq i\leq 6
by

(9.17)

\begin{split}&I_{t}^{1,n}(a):=\nu^{*}_{t}(a)\biggl(\int_{A}k_{t}(a^{\prime})d\nu^{*}_{t}(a^{\prime})-k_{t}(a)\biggr)-\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a)\biggl(\int_{A}k_{t}(a^{\prime})d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})-k_{t}(a)\biggr),\\
&I_{t}^{2,n}(a):=\biggl[\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}}\Bigl(1-e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a)}\Bigr)-k_{t}^{n}(a)\biggr]\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a),\\
&I_{t}^{3,n}(a):=\Bigl(k_{t}^{n}(a)-k_{t}(a)\Bigr)\nu_{t}^{*,n}(a),\\
&I_{t}^{4,n}(a):=\biggl[\int_{A}\Bigl(k_{t}(a^{\prime})-k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})\Bigr)d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})\biggr]\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a),\\
&I_{t}^{5,n}(a):=\biggl[\frac{e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a)}}{\lambda_{n}}\biggl(1-\frac{1}{\int_{A}e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})}d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})}+\lambda_{n}\int_{A}k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})\biggr)\biggr]\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a),\\
&I_{t}^{6,n}(a):=\biggl[\Bigl(1-e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a)}\Bigr)\int_{A}k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})\biggr]\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a).\end{split}

Proof.

Let
t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]
and
a\in A
. Recalling the expression (
3.5
) from Lemma
3.5
this gives

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}}\bigl(\Gamma[\bm{\nu}^{n}]_{t}(a)-\nu_{t}^{*,n}(a)\bigr)+\nu_{t}^{*}(a)(k_{t}(a)-c_{t})\\
&=-\frac{\nu_{t}^{*,n}(a)}{\lambda_{n}}\biggl(1-\frac{e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a)}}{\int_{A}e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})}d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})}\biggr)-\biggl(\int_{A}k_{t}(a^{\prime})d\nu^{*}_{t}(a^{\prime})-k_{t}(a)\biggr)\nu^{*}_{t}(a).\end{split}

It is then standard algebra to check that
the second line above is equal to the sum
-\sum_{i=1}^{6}I_{t}^{i,n}(a)
, i.e.

(9.18)

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}}\bigl(\Gamma[\bm{\nu}^{n}]_{t}(a)-\nu_{t}^{*,n}(a)\bigr)+\nu_{t}^{*}(a)(k_{t}(a)-c_{t})=-\sum_{i=1}^{6}I_{t}^{i,n}(a).\end{split}

and the result easily follows.
∎

Lemma 9.2
.

In the setting of Proposition
3.7
, we have the following convergence for
\bm{I}^{1,n},\bm{I}^{2,n},\bm{I}^{5,n}
and
\bm{I}^{6,n}

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}}^{T}\biggl(\int_{A}\Bigl[\bigl|I_{t}^{1,n}(a)\bigr|+\bigl|I_{t}^{2,n}(a)\bigr|+\bigl|I_{t}^{5,n}(a)\bigr|+\bigl|I_{t}^{6,n}(a)\bigr|\Bigr]da\biggr)^{2}dt=0.

Proof.

We first address the convergence of
({\bm{I}}^{1,n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
. Lemma
2.15

guarantees that

(\bm{\nu}^{*,n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
converges strongly toward
\bm{\nu}^{*}
, from which we easily deduce that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}}^{T}\biggl(\int_{A}\bigl|I_{t}^{1,n}(a)\bigr|da\biggr)^{2}dt=0.

We now address

({\bm{I}}^{2,n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}

and

({\bm{I}}^{5,n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
.
We first notice from Lemma
3.3
and Proposition
3.6
that there is

C
such that, for any

n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

t\in[t_{0}^{n},T]

and
a\in A
,

|k^{n}_{t}(a)|\leq C(1+|a|^{2})
, and
for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
and
a\in A
,
|k_{t}(a)|\leq C(1+|a|^{2})
.

For
{\bm{I}}^{2,n}
, Taylor formula yields

(9.19)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}}\Bigl(1-e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a)}\Bigr)-k_{t}^{n}(a)\Bigr|\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a)&\leq\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\lambda_{n}|k_{t}^{n}(a)|^{2}\sup_{\theta\in[0,1]}e^{-\theta\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a)}\Bigr)\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a)\\
&\leq\lambda_{n}|k_{t}^{n}(a)|^{2}\Bigl(1+e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a)}\Bigr)\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a).\end{split}

Using Proposition

2.9
,
there is no difficulty in integrating with respect to
a
, squaring and then integrating in
time. Since
(\lambda_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
tends
to
0
, we deduce that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}}^{T}\biggl(\int_{A}\bigl|I_{t}^{2,n}(a)\bigr|da\biggr)^{2}dt=0.

We now address
{\bm{I}}^{5,n}
.
First,
by integrating
(
9.19
)
with respect to
a
and then multiplying by
\lambda_{n}
, we notice that

\biggl|\int_{A}e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})}d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})-1+\lambda_{n}\int_{A}k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})\biggr|\leq C\lambda_{n}^{2}\int_{A}\bigl(1+|a|^{4}\bigr)e^{\lambda_{n}|a|^{2}}d\nu_{t}^{*,n}(a)\leq C\lambda_{n}^{2},

for a constant
C
independent of

n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]
. Then,
for a new value of
C
,

\biggl|\biggl(\int_{A}e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})}d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})\biggr)^{-1}-1-\lambda_{n}\int_{A}k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})\biggr|\leq C\lambda_{n}^{2}.

Dividing by

\lambda_{n}
,
we get

\Bigl|\frac{1}{\lambda_{n}}\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{\int_{A}e^{-\lambda_{n}k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})}d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})}\Bigr)+\int_{A}k_{t}^{n}(a^{\prime})d\nu^{*,n}_{t}(a^{\prime})\Bigr|\leq C\lambda_{n}.

Multiplying
by
\exp(-\lambda_{n}k_{t}(a))\nu^{*}_{t}(a)
, integrating in

a
, squaring and then integrating time,
we
proceed as above to
deduce that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}}^{T}\biggl(\int_{A}\bigl|I_{t}^{5,n}(a)\bigr|da\biggr)^{2}dt=0.

It thus remains to
address
{\bm{I}}^{6,n}=(I^{6,n}_{t})_{t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}\leq t\leq T}
. Recall that we can find a constant
C
such that, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\sup_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}|k^{n}_{t}(a)|\leq C(1+|a|^{2})
and then using the bound

|1-\exp(-\lambda_{n}k^{n}_{t}(a))|\leq\lambda_{n}|k^{n}_{t}(a)|e^{\lambda_{n}|k_{t}^{n}(a)|}
together with the fact that
\lambda_{n}
tends to
0
and the integrability of
\nu_{t}^{*,n}
from Lemma
3.1
, there is no difficulty in proving that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}}^{T}\biggl(\int_{A}\bigl|I_{t}^{6,n}(a)\bigr|da\biggr)^{2}dt=0.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 9.3
.

In the setting of Proposition
3.7
, we have the following convergence for
\bm{I}^{3,n},\bm{I}^{4,n}

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0}}^{T}\biggl(\int_{A}\Bigl[\bigl|I_{t}^{3,n}(a)\bigr|+\bigl|I_{t}^{4,n}(a)\bigr|\Bigr]da\biggr)^{2}dt=0.

Proof.

The convergence of the term involving
\bm{I}^{3,n}
is a direct consequence of Proposition
3.6
. Since, for all
t\in[t_{0}^{n},T]
and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\int_{A}|I_{t}^{4,n}(a)|da=\bigl|\int_{A}I_{t}^{3,n}(a)da\bigr|\leq\int_{A}|I_{t}^{3,n}(a)|da,

the result for
\bm{I}^{4,n}=(I_{t}^{4,n})_{t\in[t_{0}^{n}\vee t_{0},T]}
directly follows.

∎

Appendix A
Some Auxiliary Statements

A.1.
Lagrangian approach to the continuity and transport equations.

The goal of this section is to provide various existence, stability and regularity results for the
continuity and transport equations
(
1.3
) and
(
2.10
). This is mostly done through the (Lagrangian) representation
based on the ODE (
1.5
), which we recall below:

(A.1)

\dot{X}_{s}^{t,x}=b(X_{s}^{t,x},\nu_{s}),\quad X_{t}^{t,x}=x,\quad s\in[t,T].

Above,
b
satisfies
(i)
in
Assumption (
Regularity
)
.
The input
{\bm{\nu}}
is taken in the class
\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
defined in
Definition
2.2
.

A.1.1.
Analysis of the ODE

We start with the analysis of the ODE (
A.1
). For some initial condition
(t,x)\in[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
and some control
{\bm{\nu}}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
, we say that
(X_{s}^{t,x})_{t\leq s\leq T}
is solution to the ODE
(
A.1
) if
s\in[t,T]\mapsto X_{s}^{t,x}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}
is continuous and

\forall s\in[t,T],\quad X_{s}^{t,x}=x+\int_{t}^{s}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u})du.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
t_{0}\in[0,T]
and
{\bm{\nu}}\in{\mathcal{D}}(t_{0})
.
Then, for all
(t,x)\in[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
, there exists a unique solution to the ODE (
A.1
). For all
s\in[t,T]
, the map
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\mapsto X_{s}^{t,x}
is three times differentiable and there exists a non-decreasing function

\Lambda:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, independent of
t_{0}
and

{\bm{\nu}}
, such that

(A.2)

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\Biggl\{\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\left\|X_{s}^{t,\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{1,b}}+\sup_{t_{0}\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq T}\frac{\|X_{s_{2}}^{t,\cdot}-X_{s_{1}}^{t,\cdot}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{b}}}{\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\Biggr\}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr).

Moreover, if the coefficients
(t,x)\mapsto(b(x,\nu_{t}),\dots,\nabla_{x}^{l}b(x,\nu_{t}))
, for
l\in\{2,3\}
, are jointly continuous and bounded, then the map
(t,s,x)\mapsto X_{s}^{t,x}
(with the solution being extended to
s\in(-\infty,t)

by the solving ODE
(
A.1
) backwards
for
s<t
)
has continuous and bounded derivatives of the form
\partial^{m}_{t}\partial_{x}^{k}X_{s}^{t,x}
, with
m=0,1
,
k=0,\dots,l
and
0<m+k\leq l
.

Remark A.2
.

Notice that, for any
l\in\{0,1,2,3\}
it follows from the assumptions on
b
and its derivatives that a sufficient condition for
(t,x)\mapsto\nabla_{x}^{l}b(x,\nu_{t})
to be bounded is to require
t\mapsto\int_{A}(1+|a|^{l+1})d|\nu_{t}|(a)
to be bounded while a sufficient condition for
(t,x)\mapsto\nabla_{x}^{l}b(x,\nu_{t})
to be jointly continuous is to require
t\mapsto\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{l+1}}
to be continuous.

Proof.

Step 1.

We first address the solvability of
(
A.1
).
By (i) in
Assumption (
Regularity
)
,
we notice that, for Lebesgue almost every
t
in
[t_{0},T]
,
the vector field
x\mapsto b(x,\nu_{t})
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{3}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with

(A.3)

\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{b}}dt\leq C\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{4})d|\nu_{t}|(a)dt<+\infty.

Therefore, we
can apply Cauchy-Lipschitz theorem and deduce that
the ODE
(
A.1
) is uniquely solvable.

Step 2.

We now turn to the proof of the space regularity estimate in
(
A.2
).
We start with the following bound (for
s\in[t,T]
):

|X_{s}^{t,x}|\leq|x|+\biggl|\int_{t}^{s}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u})du\biggr|\\
\leq|x|+C\int_{s}^{t}\int_{A}(1+|a|)d|\nu_{u}|(a)du,

and the right-hand side can be easily bounded in terms of the fourth moment of

|{\bm{\nu}}|
. This gives the bound for the first term in
(
A.2
) when the
{\mathcal{C}}^{3}_{1,b}
-norm is replaced
the
{\mathcal{C}}^{0}_{1}
-norm.

Moreover, by
(
A.3
), it is a standard fact that, for each
s\in[t,T]
, the mapping
x\mapsto X_{s}^{t,x}

is three times differentiable.
Differentiating the flow, we have

(A.4)

\nabla_{x}X_{s}^{t,x}=I_{d}+\int_{t}^{s}\nabla_{x}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u})\nabla_{x}X_{u}^{t,x}du\quad s\in[t,T],

and the growth assumption on
\nabla_{x}b
gives

|\nabla_{x}X_{s}^{t,x}|\leq C+C\int_{t}^{s}\biggl(|\nabla_{x}X_{u}^{t,x}|\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\nu|_{u}(a)\biggr)du.

By Grönwall’s lemma,
the left-hand side can be estimated in terms of the fourth moment of

|{\bm{\nu}}|
.

We can proceed in the same way for the higher-order derivatives.
Taking
l\in\{2,3\}

and assuming that we have a bound for

\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\|\nabla_{x}X_{s}^{t,\cdot}\|_{{\mathcal{C}}_{b}^{l-1}}
,
we can find a universal constant
c>0
such that

\displaystyle|\nabla_{x}^{l}X_{s}^{t,x}|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{s}|\nabla_{x}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u})||\nabla_{x}^{l}X_{u}^{t,x}|du+c\sum_{k=2}^{l}\sum_{\underset{i_{1}+\cdots+i_{k}=l}{1\leq i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{k}}}\int_{s}^{t}|\nabla_{x}^{k}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u})|\prod_{j=1}^{k}|\nabla_{x}^{i_{j}}X_{u}^{t,x}|du

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{t}^{s}\biggl(|\nabla_{x}^{l}X_{u}^{t,x}|\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\nu_{u}|(a)\biggr)du

\displaystyle\quad+C\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}X_{s}^{t,\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{b}}\sum_{k=2}^{l}\int_{s}^{t}\int_{A}\bigl(1+|a|^{k+1}\bigr)d|\nu_{u}|(a)du,

for a constant
C
depending on the parameters in (i) in
Assumption (
Regularity
)
.
Using Grönwall’s lemma together with
the fact that
k+1
above is less than 4,
we derive the first bound in
(
A.2
).

Step 3.
We
now prove the time regularity estimate in (
A.2
).
It follows from the following inequality, which is itself a consequence of
(
6.1
):

\begin{split}|X_{s_{2}}^{t,x}-X_{s_{1}}^{t,x}|&\leq\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}|b(X_{s}^{t,x},\nu_{s})|ds\\
&\leq C\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|)d|\nu_{s}|(a)ds\leq C\sqrt{|s_{2}-s_{1}|}\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})},\end{split}

which holds true for all
s_{1},s_{2}\in[t,T]
with
s_{1}<s_{2}
. Similarly, recalling (
A.4
) we get

\displaystyle\bigl|\nabla_{x}X_{s_{2}}^{t,x}-\nabla_{x}X_{s_{1}}^{t,x}\bigr|\leq\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\bigl|\nabla_{x}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u})\nabla_{x}X_{u}^{t,x}\bigr|du

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{u\in[s_{1},s_{2}]}\left\|X_{u}^{t,\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1,b}}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}(1+|a|^{2})d|\nu_{u}|(a)du\leq C\sup_{u\in[s_{1},s_{2}]}\left\|X_{u}^{t,\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1,b}}\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}}\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}.

Combined with
Step 2
, this completes the proof of
(
A.2
).

Step 4.

As for the last claim in the statement it follows from

[
Hartman
, Part V, Chapter 4, Theorem 4.1 & Corollary 4.1,]
.
∎

A.1.2.
Regularizing the vector field

The analysis of the continuity
and transport equations
(
1.3
)
and (
2.10
) relies on the following regularization argument.

We start by extending
\bm{\nu}
to
\mathbb{R}
by setting

\overline{\nu}_{t}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\nu_{t}&\mbox{ if }t\in[t_{0},T],\\
0&\mbox{ otherwise. }\end{array}\right.

The collection
(\overline{\nu}_{t})_{t\in{\mathbb{R}}}
is denoted by
\overline{\bm{\nu}}.

Then we take a smooth, even, density
\rho:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
and let for all

n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
\rho^{n}:=n\rho(n\cdot)
.
For a fixed
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
,
we define

(A.5)

\bm{\nu}^{n}:=\rho^{n}*\overline{\bm{\nu}}

over
\mathbb{R}
. Equivalently, we have for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
and all Borel subset
B
of
A
,

\nu_{t}^{n}(B)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho^{n}(t-s)\overline{\nu}_{s}(B)ds=\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\rho^{n}(t-s)\nu_{s}(B)ds.

By restricting
\bm{\nu}^{n}
to
[t_{0},T]
, we can see it as an element of
\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
. Then
\bm{\nu}^{n}
satisfies the following results:

Lemma A.3
.

Take
\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
and consider

(\bm{\nu}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
as given by
(
A.5
). Then we have

(A.6)

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\left\|\bm{\nu}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\leq\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})},

as well as the uniform integrability condition

(A.7)

\lim_{K\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{4})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\nu_{t}^{n}|(a)dt=0.

Proof.

We fix some
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
. We observe that, for almost every
t\in[t_{0},T]
and all Borel subset
B
of
A
,

|\nu_{t}^{n}|(B)\leq\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\rho^{n}(t-s)\bigl[|\nu_{s}|(B)\bigr]ds.

The above inequality can be easily extended from indicator functions of Borel subsets to positive measurable functions. Therefore, for all
t_{1}<t_{2}\in[t_{0},T]
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
K>0
,
we get, by a simple change of variable,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{k})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\nu_{t}^{n}|(a)dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\rho^{n}(t-s)\int_{A}(1+|a|^{k})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\nu_{s}|(a)dsdt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{t_{0}-t}^{T-t}\rho^{n}(u)\int_{A}(1+|a|^{k})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\nu_{u+t}|(a)dudt,

and then, we deduce the trivial bound

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{k})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\nu_{t}^{n}|(a)dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho^{n}(u)\int_{A}(1+|a|^{k})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\overline{\nu}_{u+t}|(a)dudt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho^{n}(u)\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{k})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\overline{\nu}_{u+t}|(a)dtdu

from which it comes, since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho^{n}(u)du=1
,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{k})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\nu_{t}^{n}|(a)dt

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{k})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\overline{\nu}_{u+t}|(a)dt.

Now, for
k=2
and
K=0
we have

\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\overline{\nu}_{t+u}|(a)du\leq\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}\sup_{s_{1}<s_{2}\in[t_{0},T]}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}}}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{2})d|\nu_{t}|(a)dt,

where we used that
\overline{\bm{\nu}}=0
outside
[t_{0},T]\times A
and, for
k=4
,

\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{4})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\overline{\nu}_{t+u}|(a)dt=\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{4})\mathbf{1}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|\nu_{t}|(a)dt.

Combined together we get both (
A.6
) and (
A.7
).
∎

We use the above statement to derive the following properties on
the collection of vector fields

((t,x)\mapsto b(x,\nu_{t}^{n}))_{n\geq 1}
:

Lemma A.4
.

Take
\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
and consider

(\bm{\nu}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
as given by
(
A.5
). Then, for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
, the vector field
(t,x)\mapsto b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})
is smooth in the sense that all the derivatives
\partial_{t}^{m}\nabla_{x}^{k}[(t,x)\mapsto b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})]
with
m\geq 0
and
0\leq k\leq 3
are continuous and bounded. We also have the estimate

(A.8)

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\biggl\{\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{b}}dt+\sup_{t_{1}<t_{2}\in[t_{0},T]}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{b}}dt\biggr\}\leq C\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}.

Moreover, the vector fields
((t,x)\mapsto b(x,\nu_{t}^{n}))_{n\geq 1}
approximate
(t,x)\mapsto b(x,\nu_{t})
in the following sense:

(A.9)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{1}}dt=0.

Proof.

Step 1.
For a fixed
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
, we reformulate

(t,x)\mapsto b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})

in the form

b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})=\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\rho^{n}(t-s)\biggl[\int_{A}b(x,a)d\nu_{s}(a)\biggr]ds,\quad t\in[t_{0},T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}.

The regularity of
(t,x)\mapsto b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})
follows in a straightforward way from the regularity of
b
and the growth assumption on
\nabla^{k}_{x}b(x,a)
for
0\leq k\leq 3
(see
Assumption (
Regularity
)
) together with the integrability
properties of
\bm{\nu}
(see
Definition
2.2
). Moreover, for any integer
k\in\{0,1,2,3\}
,

\forall t\in[t_{0},T],\quad\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k}_{b}}\leq C\int_{A}\bigl(1+|a|^{k+1}\bigr)d|\nu_{t}^{n}|(a),

from which we deduce (
A.8
).

Step 2.

We now turn to the proof of
(
A.9
).
For this, we introduce another smooth density
\zeta:A\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
with compact support and define, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
\zeta^{m}(a)=m^{d^{\prime}}\zeta(m\,\cdot)
. For
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we let

\nu^{n,m}_{t}:=\zeta^{m}*\nu_{t}^{n},\quad\quad\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m}:=\zeta^{m}*\overline{\nu}_{t},

where the convolution is taken with respect to the
a
-variable.
For every
t\in\mathbb{R}
, the measures
\nu_{t}^{n,m}
and
\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m}
have densities with respect to the Lebesgue measure over
A
and these densities are still denoted
a\mapsto\nu_{t}^{n,m}(a)
and
a\mapsto\nu_{t}^{m}(a)
respectively. Moreover, for all

t\in{\mathbb{R}}

and
Borel subset
B
of
A
,

(A.10)

\begin{split}\nu_{t}^{n,m}(B)=\int_{A}\zeta^{m}(a)\nu_{t}^{n}(B-a)da&=\int_{A}\biggl(\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\zeta^{m}(a)\rho^{n}(t-s)\nu_{s}(B-a)ds\biggr)da\\
&=\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\biggl(\int_{A}\zeta^{m}(a)\nu_{s}(B-a)da\biggr)\rho^{n}(t-s)ds\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho^{n}(t-s)\overline{\nu}^{m}_{s}(B)ds=\bigl(\rho^{n}*\overline{\nu}^{m}(B)\bigr)_{t},\end{split}

which permits to identify

\nu_{t}^{n,m}
and
(\rho^{n}*\overline{\nu}^{m})_{t}
(with the latter being interpreted as a Pettis integral, even though we do not use this notion below).

For all
(t,a)\in[t_{0},T]
,
m,n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}

and
k\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl|\nabla_{x}^{k}b(x,\nu_{t}^{n,m})-\nabla^{k}_{x}b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})\bigr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\int_{A}\bigl[\zeta^{m}*\nabla_{x}^{k}b(x,\cdot)(a)-\nabla_{x}^{k}b(x,a)\bigr]d\nu_{t}^{n}(a)\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{m}(1+|x|)\int_{A}(1+|a|^{k+1})d|\nu_{t}^{n}|(a),

where we used the growth assumption on
\nabla_{x}^{k}\nabla_{a}b
in the last line (see
Assumption (
Regularity
)
). It then follows from (
A.6
) in Lemma
A.3
that

(A.11)

\lim_{m\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n,m})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{1}}dt=0.

Similarly, we have

(A.12)

\lim_{m\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{1}}dt=0.

Now, for fixed
k\in\{1,2,3\}
and
m,n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, we deduce from
(
A.10
) that, for all
(t,x)\in[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
,

\displaystyle\bigl|\nabla_{x}^{k}b(x,\nu_{t}^{n,m})-\nabla_{x}^{k}b(x,\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m})\bigr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho(s)\biggl[\int_{A}\nabla_{x}^{k}b(x,a)\bigl[\overline{\nu}_{t-s/n}^{m}(a)-\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m}(a)\bigr]da\biggr]ds\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho(s)\biggl(\int_{A}(1+|a|^{k+1})\bigl|\overline{\nu}^{m}_{t-s/n}(a)-\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m}(a)\bigr|da\biggr)ds

for some
C>0
independent of
(t,x)\in[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
and
m,n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. As a consequence, we get

(A.13)

\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n,m})-b(\cdot,\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{b}}dt\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}}\rho(s)\biggl\{\int_{A}(1+|a|^{4})\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl|\overline{\nu}^{m}_{t-s/n}(a)-\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m}(a)\bigr|dt\biggr)da\biggr\}ds.

Now, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
a\in A
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle(1+|a|^{4})|\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m}(a)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A}(1+|a|^{4})\zeta^{m}(a-a^{\prime})d|\overline{\nu}_{t}|(a^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{A}|a-a^{\prime}|^{4}\zeta^{m}(a-a^{\prime})d|\overline{\nu}_{t}|(a^{\prime})+4\int_{A}(1+|a^{\prime}|^{4})\zeta^{m}(a-a^{\prime})d|\overline{\nu}_{t}|(a^{\prime})

(A.14)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m}\int_{A}(1+|a^{\prime}|^{4})d|\overline{\nu}_{t}|(a^{\prime}),

for some
C_{m}>0
independent from
(t,a)\in\mathbb{R}\times A
, but depending on
m
. In particular, since
\bm{\nu}
belongs to
\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
, for every
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and every
a\in A
, the map
t\mapsto\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m}(a)
belongs to
L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
. By definition of
\overline{\bm{\nu}}
, it also has compact support in
[t_{0},T]
. By a simple approximation (in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R})
) by uniformly continuous functions, we deduce that, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and

(s,a)\in\mathbb{R}\times A
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl|\overline{\nu}^{m}_{t-s/n}(a)-\overline{\nu}^{m}_{t}(a)\bigr|dt=0.

Using the bound (
A.14
) together with Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and getting back to (
A.13
) we deduce that, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

(A.15)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n,m})-b(\cdot,\overline{\nu}_{t}^{m})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{b}}dt=0.

Combining together (
A.11
), (
A.12
) and (
A.15
), this leads to

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{1}}dt=0,

which completes the proof of (
A.9
).
∎

Corollary A.5
.

Let
t_{0}\in[0,T]
and
{\bm{\nu}}\in{\mathcal{D}}(t_{0})
. For

({\bm{\nu}}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
defined in (
A.5
), let, for each

n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
,

(X^{n,t,x}_{s})_{t\leq s\leq T}

be the solution
to the ODE
(
A.1
)
driven by
{\bm{\nu}}^{n}
.
Then, we have

(A.16)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\left\|X_{s}^{t,\cdot}-X_{s}^{n,t,\cdot}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{1}}=0.

Proof.

Step 1.

Following
(
A.3
) and
using
Grönwall’s lemma, we can find
a constant
C
such that, for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}

and
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{s\in[t,T]}|X_{s}^{t,x}-X_{s}^{n,t,x}|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\biggl|\int_{t}^{s}b\bigl(X_{r}^{t,x},\nu_{r}^{n}-\nu_{r}\bigr)dr\biggr|.

\displaystyle\leq C(1+|x|)\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\biggl[\frac{1+|X^{t,z}_{s}|}{1+|z|}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{r}^{n})-b(\cdot,\nu_{r})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{1}}dr\biggr].

Using the linear growth of
X_{r}^{t,x}
from (
A.2
) of Proposition
A.1
as well as the convergence of
b(\cdot,\nu^{n})
to
b(\cdot,\nu)
from Lemma
A.9
we deduce that

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}}\frac{|X_{s}^{t,x}-X_{s}^{n,t,x}|}{1+|x|}=0.

This proves
(
A.16
)
when the sum over

k
therein is in fact restricted to

k=0
.

Step 2.

We claim that, by the same argument, we can handle the convergence of
\nabla^{k}_{x}X_{s}^{n,t,x}
for
k=1,2,3
. Since, by Proposition
A.1
and Lemma
A.3
,
\|\nabla_{x}X_{s}^{n,t,\cdot}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{b}}
is bounded independently of
t,s
and
n
, it suffices to prove that convergence holds uniformly over bounded subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
. In fact, the main difficulty is the case of the third derivative in
x
, which we prove now under the assumption that,
(\nabla_{x}X_{s}^{t,n})_{n\geq 1}
and
(\nabla^{2}_{x}X_{s}^{t,n})_{n\geq 1}
converge uniformly over compact subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
(uniformly in
t\in[t_{0},T]
and
s\in[t,T]
as well) to
\nabla_{x}X_{s}^{t,x}
and
\nabla_{x}^{2}X_{s}^{t,x}
respectively. Following the first step in the proof of Proposition
A.1
, we can find a constant
C
such that, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
and
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}
,

\begin{split}&\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\bigl|\nabla_{x}^{3}X_{s}^{n,t,x}-\nabla_{x}^{3}X_{s}^{t,x}\bigr|\\
&\leq C\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\biggl|\int_{t}^{s}\bigl[\nabla_{x}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u})-\nabla_{x}b(X_{u}^{n,t,x},\nu_{u}^{n})\bigr]\otimes\nabla_{x}^{3}X_{u}^{t,x}du\biggr|\\
&\hskip 5.0pt+C\sum_{k=2,3}\sum_{\underset{i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}=3}{1\leq i_{1}\leq\dots\leq i_{k}}}\sup_{s\in[t,T]}\biggl|\int_{t}^{s}\Bigl(\nabla_{x}^{k}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\nabla_{x}^{i_{j}}X_{u}^{t,x}-\nabla_{x}^{k}b(X_{u}^{n,t,x},\nu_{u}^{n})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\nabla_{x}^{i_{j}}X_{u}^{n,t,x}\Bigr)du\biggr|\\
&=:C\bigl(T_{1}^{n}(t,s,x)+T_{2}^{n}(t,s,x)\bigr).\end{split}

We explain how to handle
T_{2}^{n}(t,s,x)
. The term
T_{1}^{n}(t,s,x)
can be handled in the same way. For

k\in\{2,3\}
and
(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})\in\{1,\dots,k-1\}

with
i_{1}\leq\dots\leq i_{k}
and
i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}=3
, we write

\begin{split}&\int_{t}^{s}\Bigl(\nabla_{x}^{k}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\nabla_{x}^{i_{j}}X_{u}^{t,x}-\nabla_{x}^{k}b(X_{u}^{n,t,x},\nu_{u}^{n})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\nabla_{x}^{i_{j}}X_{u}^{n,t,x}\Bigr)du\\
&=\int_{t}^{s}\Bigl(\nabla_{x}^{k}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u}-\nu_{u}^{n})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\nabla_{x}^{i_{j}}X_{u}^{t,x}\Bigr)du\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t}^{s}\Bigl(\nabla_{x}^{k}b(X_{u}^{t,x},\nu_{u}^{n})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\nabla_{x}^{i_{j}}X_{u}^{t,x}-\nabla_{x}^{k}b(X_{u}^{n,t,x},\nu_{u}^{n})\otimes\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\nabla_{x}^{i_{j}}X_{u}^{n,t,x}\Bigr)du\\
&=:T_{2,1}^{n,k,(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})}(t,s,x)+T_{2,2}^{n,k,(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})}(t,s,x).\end{split}

Using the bounds available for
the derivatives of

b
(see (i) in
Assumption (
Regularity
)
) and
the derivatives of

X^{t,\cdot}_{u}

and
X^{n,t,\cdot}_{u}

(see Proposition

A.1
), we deduce that, for any
K>0
,

\begin{split}&\bigl|T_{2,2}^{n,k,(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})}(t,s,x)\bigr|\\
&\leq\int_{t}^{T}\biggl(\int_{A}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|a|\leq K\}}\Bigl|\nabla_{x}^{k}b(X_{u}^{t,x},a)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\nabla_{x}^{i_{j}}X_{u}^{t,x}-\nabla_{x}^{k}b(X_{u}^{n,t,x},a)\otimes\bigotimes_{j=1}^{k}\nabla_{x}^{i_{j}}X_{u}^{n,t,x}\Bigr|d|\nu_{u}^{n}|(a)\biggr)du\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+C\int_{t}^{T}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{4}){\mathbf{1}}_{\{|a|\geq K\}}d|{\bm{\nu}}^{n}|(t,a),\end{split}

the constant
C
in the last line being independent of
(t,s,x,n)
.
By (
A.7
) in
Lemma

A.9
, the second term in the right-hand side tends to
0
as

K
tends to
\infty
, uniformly in
n\geq 1
.
Then, for a given
K
we can use the continuity of
\nabla_{x}^{k}b
and the fact that
(
A.16
) holds true for the derivatives of order 0, 1 and 2 to deduce that the first term in the right-hand side tends to
0
as
n
tends to
\infty
, uniformly with respect to
x
in compact subsets.

Step 3.
We now address

T_{2,1}^{n,k,(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})}(t,s,x)
that can be estimated as follows, thanks to Proposition
A.1
,

\displaystyle\bigl|T_{2,1}^{n,k,(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})}(t,s,x)\bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C(1+|x|)\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{u})-b(\cdot,\nu_{u}^{n})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{1}}du.

The proof is easily completed using Lemma
A.9
.
∎

A.1.3.
Well-posedness of the Continuity and Transport Equations

Lemma A.6
.

Take
t_{0},t_{1}\in[0,T]
with
t_{1}>t_{0}
and
\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
. Assume moreover that
[t_{0},t_{1}]\ni t\mapsto\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|}(A)
is continuous. Then, for any
p\geq q\geq 0
and any
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p,q}
there is a unique classical solution
\varphi:[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
to the backward transport equation

(A.17)

-\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}-b(x,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}=0\quad\mbox{ in }[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\varphi_{t_{1}}=\phi\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}.

It is given by
\varphi_{t}(x,y)=\phi(X_{t_{1}}^{t,x},y)
, for all
(t,x,y)\in[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
, where
(X_{s}^{t,x})_{s\in[t,t_{1}]}
is the solution to the ODE

\dot{X}_{s}^{t,x}=b(X_{s}^{t,x},\nu_{s})\quad s\in[t,t_{1}],\quad X_{t}^{t,x}=x.

Moreover,
there exists a non-decreasing function

\Lambda:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, independent of
t_{0}
,
\phi
and

{\bm{\nu}}
, such that

(A.18)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{p}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{p}},

(A.19)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|\nabla\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{q}}+\sup_{t_{2}<t_{3}\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\frac{\left\|\varphi_{t_{3}}-\varphi_{t_{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{q}}}{\sqrt{t_{3}-t_{2}}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\left\|\nabla\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{q}}.

If we also assume that, for
l=2
or
l=3
,
\phi
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{l}_{p,q}
and
[t_{0},t_{1}]\ni t\mapsto\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{l}}(A)
is continuous, then
\phi
has continuous derivatives of the form
\partial_{t}^{m}\nabla^{k}_{x}\varphi
with
m=0,1
,
k=0,\dots,l
and
m+k\leq l
.
Moreover, for a possibly new choice of
\Lambda
, we have the estimates

(A.20)

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}+\sup_{t_{2}<t_{2}\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\frac{\left\|\varphi_{t_{3}}-\varphi_{t_{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{q}}}{\sqrt{t_{3}-t_{2}}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\left\|\nabla\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}.

In that case,
\partial_{t}\varphi
and
\nabla_{x}\varphi
also belong to
\mathcal{C}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathcal{C}^{l-2}_{q})\cap\mathcal{L}^{\infty}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q})
.

Remark A.7
.

Notice that, for any
\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
and any
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the regularization
\bm{\nu}^{n}
from Lemma
A.9
satisfies the continuity condition required in Lemma
A.6
.

Remark A.8
.

As an application of Lemma
A.6
(with
l=3
), we find that, if
[t_{0},T]\ni t\mapsto\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{3}}(A)
is continuous and
\phi
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2,1}
then the solution
\varphi:[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
with terminal condition
\phi
and control
\bm{\nu}
is three times continuously differentiable and satisfies

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2,1}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2,1}}.

Remark A.9
.

In particular, if
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{3}_{2,1}
and
\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
such that
[t_{0},T]\ni t\mapsto\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{3}}(A)
is continuous, then the solution
\varphi
satisfies

(A.21)

\varphi\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})\cap\mathcal{L}^{\infty}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})

(A.22)

\partial_{t}\varphi,\nabla_{x}\varphi\in\mathcal{C}\bigl([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2})\cap\mathcal{L}^{\infty}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathcal{C}^{2}_{1})

This is therefore an admissible test function for the equation for
\bm{\rho}
.

Proof of Lemma
A.6
.

When
\phi
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}
, both the well-posedness of the equation and the representation formula follow directly from classical theory. Specifically, due to the continuity of the map
t\mapsto\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|}(A)
for
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
, we can apply Remark
A.2
. This implies that the vector field
(t,x)\mapsto b(x,\nu_{t})
, which is already Lipschitz in
(x,y)
with a time-integrable Lipschitz constant, is jointly continuous and bounded.
When
\phi
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{l}_{p,q}
,
the higher regularity properties of

\varphi
follow from the explicit representation formula for the latter together with Proposition
A.1
and Remark
A.2
.

The estimate in the first line of
(
A.18
) and the bounds for the first terms in
the left-hand sides of
(
A.19
)
and
(
A.20
) also follow from the explicit formula and Proposition
A.1
.

To handle the second terms in
the left-hand sides of
(
A.19
)
and
(
A.20
),
we use
Assumption (
Regularity
)
together with the fact that
\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}\leq\Lambda(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})})\left\|\nabla\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}
and proceed as follows. With
l
being equal to 1, 2 we have, for any
t_{3}>t_{2}\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\varphi_{t_{3}}-\varphi_{t_{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{3}}b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}dt\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}\leq\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{3}}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}dt

(A.23)

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{3}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{l})d|\nu_{t}|(a)dt

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{t_{3}-t_{2}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})},

for some
C>0
depending only on the vector field
b
.

For
l=3
,
\nabla_{x}\varphi
belongs to
\mathcal{C}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathcal{C}_{q}^{1})
as a consequence of the continuity of
[t_{0},t_{1}]\ni t\mapsto\nu_{t}\in\mathcal{M}_{1+|a|^{3}}(A)
. Indeed following the derivation of (
A.23
) we have, for any
t_{3}>t_{2}\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\varphi_{t_{3}}-\varphi_{t_{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{q}}\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{3}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{3})d|\nu_{t}|(a)dt

\displaystyle\leq C(t_{3}-t_{2})\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{q}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{3})d|\nu_{t}|(a)

for some
C>0
depending only on
b
.

It remains to prove the regularity of
\partial_{t}\varphi
when
l=2,3
. Using
the equation (
A.17
) for
\partial_{t}\varphi
, we have

\left\|\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}=\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}\leq C\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{l})d|\nu_{t}|(a),

and,

\displaystyle\bigl\|\partial_{t}\varphi_{t_{3}}-\partial_{t}\varphi_{t_{2}}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-2}_{q}}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t_{3}})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{3}}-b(\cdot,\nu_{t_{2}})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{2}}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}_{q}^{l-2}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl\|[b(\cdot,\nu_{t_{3}})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t_{2}})]\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{3}}\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-2}_{q}}+\bigl\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t_{2}})\cdot\nabla_{x}(\varphi_{t_{3}}-\varphi_{t_{2}})\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-2}_{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t_{3}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-2}_{q}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{l-1})d|\nu_{t_{3}}-\nu_{t_{2}}|(a)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+C\left\|\nabla_{x}(\varphi_{t_{3}}-\varphi_{t_{2}})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-2}_{q}}\int_{A}(1+|a|^{l-1})d|\nu_{t_{2}}|(a),

and we conclude using the bounds on
\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}
and
\left\|\varphi_{t_{3}}-\varphi_{t_{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}
.
This completes the proof of the regularity of the lemma.
∎

Regarding
the analysis of
(
2.8
), we now turn to the following well-posedness result for the continuity equation, previously stated in Proposition

2.4
:

Lemma A.10
.

Take
{\bm{\nu}}=(\nu_{t})_{t_{0}\leq t\leq T}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
. Then, for any given
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
, there is a unique distributional solution
{\bm{\gamma}}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}))
to the continuity equation

(A.24)

\partial_{t}\gamma_{t}+\operatorname{div}_{x}(b(x,\nu_{t})\gamma_{t})=0\quad\mbox{ in }(t_{0},T)\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\gamma(t_{0})=\gamma_{0}.

It is given by

(\gamma_{t}=(X_{t}^{t_{0},\cdot},i_{d})\#\gamma_{0})_{t_{0}\leq t\leq T}

where
(X_{t}^{t_{0},\cdot})_{t_{0}\leq t\leq T}

is the flow of
(
1.5
).

Moreover, there exists a non-decreasing function
\Lambda:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, independent of
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})
and

{\bm{\nu}}
, such that, for each
p\geq 1
such that
\gamma_{0}
belongs to
\mathcal{P}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
,

(A.25)

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\frac{\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\bigl(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\bigr)^{p/2}d\gamma_{t}(x,y)\Bigr)^{1/p}}{1+\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\bigl(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\bigr)^{p/2}d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\Bigr)^{1/p}}+\sup_{t\neq s\in[t_{0},T]}\frac{d_{p}(\gamma_{t},\gamma_{s})}{\sqrt{|t-s|}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr).

Proof.

For existence, we easily check
(with the notation of Proposition
A.1
) that
the flow of probability measures
(\gamma_{t}=(X_{t}^{t_{0},\cdot},i_{d})\#\gamma_{0})_{t_{0}\leq t\leq T}
is a solution. As a consequence of
(
A.2
), we have, for each
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\bigl(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\bigr)^{p/2}d\gamma_{t}(x,y)\Bigr)^{1/p}

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\bigl(\bigl|X_{t}^{t_{0},x}\bigr|^{2}+|y|^{2}\bigr)^{p/2}d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\Bigr)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\bigl(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}+\Lambda^{2}(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})})\bigr)^{p/2}d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\Bigr)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\Bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\bigl(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2}\bigr)^{p/2}d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\Bigr)^{1/p}+\sqrt{2}\Lambda(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}),

where we used Minkowski’s inequality at the last line. Moreover,

d_{p}(\gamma_{t},\gamma_{s})\leq\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}|X_{t}^{t_{0},x_{0}}-X_{s}^{t_{0},x_{0}}|^{p}d\gamma_{0}(x_{0},y_{0})\biggr)^{1/p}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\sqrt{|t-s|},

which proves
(
A.24
)
and
(
A.25
).

For uniqueness, we consider two (distributional) solutions
{\bm{\gamma}}^{1},{\bm{\gamma}}^{2}
in

{\mathcal{C}}([t_{0},T],{\mathcal{P}}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}))
. For given
t_{1}\in(t_{0},T]
and
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
, we consider
({\bm{\nu}}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
the approximation of
{\bm{\nu}}
given by
(
A.5
).
Recalling Lemma
A.6
, we find that for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
, there exists a (unique) solution
{\varphi}^{n}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{1,2}([t_{0},T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
(one continuous time derivative and two continuous space derivatives)
to the transport equation

-\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}^{n}-b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}=0\quad\mbox{ in }[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}},\quad\quad\varphi_{t_{1}}^{n}=\phi\quad\mbox{ in }\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}.

(
A.1
)
By combining Lemma
A.6

and (
A.6
) in
Lemma

A.9
,
it is easy to
find a constant
C
such that, for any
n\geq 1
,

\|\nabla_{x}\varphi^{n}\|_{{\mathcal{C}}_{b}^{0}}\leq C
.
Using the definition of a solution to the continuity equation, as given in
Subsection

2.1
(see
(
2.7
)), we obtain

(A.26)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d(\gamma_{t_{1}}^{2}-\gamma_{t_{1}}^{1})(x,y)=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\nu_{t}-\nu_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{n}(x,y)d(\gamma_{t}^{2}-\gamma_{t}^{1})(x,y)dt.

As a consequence, we get

\displaystyle\Bigl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}

\displaystyle\phi(x,y)d(\gamma_{t_{1}}^{2}-\gamma_{t_{1}}^{1})(x,y)\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigl(\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(1+|x|)d(\gamma_{t}^{1}+\gamma_{t}^{2})(x,y)\Bigr)\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n})-b(\cdot,\nu_{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{1}}dt

and we deduce from Lemma
A.9
and letting
n\rightarrow+\infty
that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d(\gamma_{t_{1}}^{2}-\gamma_{t_{1}}^{1})(x,y)=0.

Since this is true for any smooth
\phi
, we get that
\gamma^{1}_{t_{1}}=\gamma^{2}_{t_{1}}
, which implies uniqueness
because
t_{1}
is arbitrary.
∎

Using similar arguments, we now address the well-posedness of the transport equation (
A.17
), but without the continuity assumption made in the statement of
Lemma
A.6
. It will be convenient to cover the case where the terminal condition is only a
\mathcal{C}^{1}
function, in which case
t\mapsto\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x,y)
is not necessarily continuous. If
\phi
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}
for some
p\geq 0
, we say that
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
is a solution to the transport equation if, for all
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
,
(x,y)\mapsto\varphi_{t}(x,y)
is differentiable,
\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p}}
is finite and it holds, for all
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
and all
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}

\varphi_{t}(x,y)=\phi(x,y)+\int_{t}^{t_{1}}b(x,\nu_{s})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{s}(x,y)ds.

Proposition A.11
.

Take
t_{0},t_{1}\in[0,T]
, with
t_{0}<t_{1}
, and
\bm{\nu}\in\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
. Consider a terminal condition
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{p,q}
for some
p,q\geq 0
. Then, there is a unique solution
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
to the backward transport equation
(
A.17
) with terminal condition
\phi
. It is
given by
\varphi_{t}(x,y)=\phi(X_{t_{1}}^{t,x},y)
, for all
(t,x,y)\in[t_{0},t_{1}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
, where
(X_{s}^{t,x})_{s\in[t,t_{1}]}
is the solution to the ODE

\dot{X}_{s}^{t,x}=b(X_{s}^{t,x},\nu_{s})\quad s\in[t,t_{1}],\quad X_{t}^{t,x}=x.

Moreover, there exists a non-decreasing function

\Lambda:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, independent of
t_{0}
,
\phi
and

{\bm{\nu}}
, such that

\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{p}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\left\|\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{p}},\\
&\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|\nabla\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{q}}+\sup_{t_{2}<t_{3}\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\frac{\left\|\varphi_{t_{3}}-\varphi_{t_{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{q}}}{\sqrt{t_{3}-t_{2}}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\left\|\nabla\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{q}}.\end{split}

If we also assume that, for
l=2
or
l=3
,
\phi
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{l}_{p,q}
, then
\varphi_{t}
belongs to
\mathcal{C}^{l}_{p,q}
for all
t\in[t_{0},T]
, and we have, for a possibly new function
\Lambda
,

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\nabla\varphi_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}+\sup_{t_{2}<t_{3}\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\frac{\left\|\varphi_{t_{3}}-\varphi_{t_{2}}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{q}}}{\sqrt{t_{3}-t_{2}}}\leq\Lambda\bigl(\left\|\bm{\nu}\right\|_{\mathcal{D}(t_{0})}\bigr)\left\|\nabla\phi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{l-1}_{q}}.

Proof.

For
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{l}_{p,q}
, with
l=1,2,3
,
we let
\varphi_{t}(x,y):=\phi(X_{T}^{t,x},y)
. Regularizing
\bm{\nu}
into
(\bm{\nu}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
as in (
A.5
) and combining Corollary
A.5
and Lemma
A.6
we get the existence of a solution satisfying the different regularity estimates. It remains to prove uniqueness. Using the same sequence

({\bm{\nu}}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
,
we consider the continuity equation
(
1.3
) but initialized at some
t_{2}\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
from some

\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})

with a smooth compactly supported density.
Following the proof of Lemma
A.10

, we know that the solution
(when restricted to
[t_{2},t_{1}]
)
reads

(\gamma_{t}^{n}=(X_{t}^{n,t_{2},\cdot},i_{d})\#\gamma_{0})_{t_{2}\leq t\leq t_{1}}
, where
(X^{n,t_{2},x}_{t})_{t_{2}\leq t\leq t_{1}}
solves the ODE
(
A.1
)
with

{\bm{\nu}}

replaced by

{\bm{\nu}}^{n}
. In particular, each
\gamma_{t}^{n}
has a density. Denoting by
(\chi_{t}^{n,\cdot})_{t_{2}\leq t\leq t_{1}}
the inverse of the flow

(X_{t}^{n,t_{2},\cdot})_{t_{2}\leq t\leq t_{1}}
, we can write this density as (denoting the density by
\gamma_{t}^{n}
as itself):

\gamma_{t}^{n}(x,y)=\frac{\gamma_{0}(\chi_{t}^{n,x},y)}{|{\rm det}(\nabla_{x}X_{t}^{n,t_{2},\chi_{t}^{n,x}})|},\quad t\in[t_{2},t_{1}],\ (x,y)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}},

from which we deduce that

({\bm{\gamma}}_{t}^{n}(x,y))_{t_{2}\leq t\leq t_{1}}
is continuously differentiable in
t
. Then, we can justify that, for any other solution
{\varphi}^{\prime}

to the transport equation (satisfying the prescription of
Proposition
2.6
),

(A.27)

\begin{split}&\varphi^{\prime}_{t_{2}}(x,y)\gamma^{n}_{t_{2}}(x,y)-\phi(x,y)\gamma^{n}_{t_{1}}(x,y)\\
&=\lim_{|\pi|\rightarrow 0}\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\Bigl[\bigl(\varphi_{s_{i}}^{\prime}(x,y)-\varphi_{s_{i+1}}^{\prime}(x,y)\bigr)\gamma^{n}_{s_{i}}(x,y)+\varphi_{s_{i+1}}^{\prime}(x,y)\bigl(\gamma_{s_{i}}^{n}(x,y)-\gamma_{s_{i+1}}^{n}(x,y)\bigr)\Bigr]\\
&=\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}b(x,\nu_{s})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{s}^{\prime}(x,y)\gamma_{s}^{n}(x,y)ds-\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}\varphi_{s}^{\prime}(x,y)\partial_{s}{\gamma}_{s}^{n}(x,y)ds.\end{split}

where the limit is taken over the subdivision
\pi=(s_{i})_{i=1,\dots,n}
of

[t_{2},t_{1}]
(with
|\pi|
denoting the step of
\pi
). Since
{\bm{\gamma}}^{n}
is compactly supported, we can integrate in

(x,y)
. Using the continuity
equation satisfied by

{\bm{\gamma}}^{n}
and then performing an integration by parts, we obtain

(A.28)

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\varphi_{t_{1}}^{\prime}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\gamma_{t_{2}}^{n}(x,y)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\nu_{s}-\nu_{s}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{s}^{\prime}(x,y)d\gamma^{n}_{s}(x,y)ds\\
&=:S_{1}^{n}+S_{2}^{n}.\end{split}

The first term
S_{1}^{n}
is equal to
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}}}\phi(X_{t_{1}}^{n,t_{2},x},y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)
.
Therefore, it converges to

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}S_{1}^{n}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}}}\phi(X_{t_{1}}^{t_{2},x},y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}}}\varphi_{t_{2}}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y).

As for the second term, we have

\displaystyle S_{2}^{n}

\displaystyle=\biggl|\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\nu_{t}-\nu_{t}^{n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{\prime}(x,y)d\gamma^{n}_{t}(x,y)dt\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[t_{2},t_{1}]}\left\|\nabla_{x}\varphi^{\prime}_{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{q}}\sup_{t\in[t_{2},t_{1}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}(|x|^{2}+|y|^{2})^{(q+1)/2}d\gamma_{t}^{n}(x,y)\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{n}-\nu_{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{1}}dt.

Since
\gamma_{0}
is compactly supported, we easily show that the term in the middle is bounded independently of
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
and conclude that
\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}S_{2}^{n}=0.
As a consequence, for all
t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]
and all smooth and compactly supported density
\gamma_{0}
it holds

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\varphi^{\prime}_{t_{1}}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\varphi_{t_{1}}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)

from which we conclude that
\varphi^{\prime}=\varphi
and there is a unique solution to the equation.
∎

A.1.4.
Duality Relation

Lemma A.12
.

Take
t_{0}\in[0,T]
and
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
. Take as well
t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]
,
\phi\in\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}
and
\bm{\nu}^{1},\bm{\nu}^{2}
in
\mathcal{D}(t_{0})
. Let
\bm{\gamma}^{1}
be the solution to the continuity equation
(
A.24
) starting from
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})
and driven by
\bm{\nu}^{1}
, and
{\varphi}^{2}
the solution to the backward equation
(
A.17
) with terminal condition
\phi
at
t_{1}
and driven by
\bm{\nu}^{2}
. Then, we have

(A.29)

\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\gamma_{t_{1}}^{1}(x,y)&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{2}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\nu_{t}^{1}-\nu_{t}^{2})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{2}(x,y)d\gamma^{1}_{t}(x,y)dt.\end{split}

If we let
\bm{\gamma}^{2}
be the solution to the continuity equation starting from
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})
driven by
\bm{\nu}^{2}
we also have
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{2}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\gamma_{t_{1}}^{2}(x,y)
and, if
{\varphi}^{1}
is the solution to the backward transport equation with terminal condition
\phi
and control
\bm{\nu}^{1}
, we have
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\gamma_{t_{1}}^{1}(x,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\varphi^{1}_{t_{0}}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)
.

Proof.

We approximate
\bm{\nu}^{2}
by
(\bm{\nu}^{2,n})_{n\geq 1}
as in (
A.5
), and we let
({\varphi}^{2,n})_{n\geq 1}
be the corresponding solutions to (
A.17
) (with terminal condition
\phi
at
t_{1}
).
We know from Lemma
A.6
that
{\varphi}^{2,n},\partial_{t}{\varphi}^{2,n}
and
\nabla_{x}{\varphi}^{2,n}
are continuous with quadratic growth. In particular,
{\varphi}^{2,n}
is an admissible test function for the continuity equation (see (
2.7
), which easily extends to test functions with quadratic growth, thanks to (
A.25
)), and we have

(A.30)

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\gamma^{1}_{t_{1}}(x,y)\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{2,n}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\bigl\{\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}^{2,n}(x,y)+b(x,\nu_{t}^{1})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{2,n}(x,y)\bigl\}d\gamma^{1}_{t}(x,y)dt\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{2,n}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y)+\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}b(x,\nu_{t}^{1}-\nu_{t}^{2,n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{2,n}(x,y)d\gamma^{1}_{t}(x,y)dt,\end{split}

where we used the equation satisfied by
\bm{\gamma}^{1}
first and
{\varphi}^{2,n}
second. Now, for all
t\in[t_{0},T]
,
(x,y)\mapsto\varphi^{2,n}_{t}(x,y)
and
(x,y)\mapsto b(x,\nu^{1}_{t}-\nu_{t}^{2,n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{2,n}(x,y)
converge pointwise as
n\rightarrow+\infty
to
(x,y)\mapsto\varphi_{t}(x,y)
and
(x,y)\mapsto b(x,\nu_{t}^{1}-\nu_{t}^{2})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}(x,y)
respectively, see Corollary
A.5
. Moreover, by Proposition
A.11
we have the uniform bounds

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\left\|\varphi_{t_{0}}^{2,n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{2}}+\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]}\left\|b(\cdot,\nu_{t}^{1}-\nu_{t}^{2,n})\cdot\nabla_{x}\varphi_{t}^{2,n}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{0}_{2}}<+\infty

where we also used the fact that the drifts
(b(\cdot,{\bm{\nu}}^{n}))_{n\geq 1}

are uniformly bounded. Since
\bm{\gamma}^{1}
is bounded in
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}})
by Lemma
A.10
we easily obtain (
A.29
) by taking the limit
n\rightarrow+\infty
in (
A.30
) and using Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem. This proves the first part of the statement.

For the two claims in the second part of the statement, it suffices to notice from the first part that, in the special case
\bm{\nu}^{1}=\bm{\nu}^{2}
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\phi(x,y)d\gamma_{t_{1}}^{2}(x,y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{2}(x,y)d\gamma_{0}(x,y),

and similarly with the index
2
being replaced by
1
.
∎

A.1.5.
FBODE Representation

Lemma A.13
.

Let
(t_{0},\gamma_{0})\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d_{1}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{d_{2}})

and
{\bm{\nu}}\in\mathcal{A}(t_{0})
with
{\bm{u}}=(u_{t})_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
as corresponding solution to the backward equation
(
2.10
). If
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
is a probability space supporting a random variable
(X_{0},Y_{0})
with probability distribution
\gamma_{0}
and
