(X_{t})_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
is the solution to
the ODE

\dot{X}_{t}=b(X_{t},\nu_{t}),\quad t\in[t_{0},T];\quad X_{t_{0}}=X_{0},

then
(Z_{t}:=\nabla_{x}u_{t}(X_{t},Y_{0}))_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
solves the backward ODE

\dot{Z}_{t}=-\nabla_{x}b(X_{t},\nu_{t})Z_{t},\quad t\in[t_{0},T];\quad Z_{T}=\nabla_{x}L(X_{T},Y_{0}).

Proof.

Once again, we use
the regularization procedure introduced in Lemma

A.9
and consider
the same sequence
({\bm{\nu}}^{n})_{n\geq 1}

as therein.
Following Corollary

A.5
, we call, for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
,
(X_{t}^{n})_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
the solution to the ODE
(
A.1
)
starting from
X_{0}
and driven by the control
{\bm{\nu}}^{n}
.
We also denote by

(u_{t}^{n})_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
the solution to the backward transport equation
(
2.10
) driven by
{\bm{\nu}}^{n}
. We then let
Z_{t}^{n}:=\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{n}(X_{t}^{n},Y_{0})
for each
t\in[t_{0},T]
. Then,
observing that
(t,x,y)\mapsto\nabla_{x}u^{n}_{t}(x,y)

is time differentiable (see Lemma
A.6
), we can differentiate

t\in[t_{0},T]\mapsto Z_{t}^{n}
. We find

\dot{Z}_{t}^{n}=\partial_{t}\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{n}(X_{t}^{n},Y_{0})+\nabla^{2}_{xx}u_{t}^{n}(X_{t}^{n},Y_{0})\dot{X}_{t}^{n}=\partial_{t}\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{n}(X_{t}^{n},Y_{0})+\nabla_{xx}^{2}u_{t}^{n}(X_{t}^{n},Y_{0})b(X_{t}^{n},\nu_{t}^{n}).

Moreover,
differentiating in
x
the equation for
u_{t}^{n}
we find that

-\partial_{t}\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{n}(x,y)-\nabla_{x}b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{n}(x,y)-\nabla^{2}_{xx}u_{t}^{n}(x,y)b(x,\nu_{t}^{n})=0,\quad(t,x,y)\in[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}.

Therefore,

\dot{Z}_{t}^{n}=-\nabla_{x}b(X_{t}^{n},Y_{0})Z_{t}^{n},\quad t\in[t_{0},T]

which can be rewritten into

Z_{t}^{n}=\nabla_{x}L(X_{T}^{n},Y_{0})+\int_{t}^{T}\nabla_{x}b(X_{s}^{n},\nu_{s}^{n})Z_{s}^{n}ds,\quad t\in[t_{0},T].

It remains to let
n
tend to
+\infty
. To do so, we observe that, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
t\in[t_{0},T]
,

Z_{t}^{n}-Z_{t}=\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{n}(X_{t}^{n},Y_{0})-\nabla_{x}u_{t}(X_{t},Y_{0})
.
By Corollary
A.5
(and the representation formula provided by
Proposition
A.11
),

(x,y)\mapsto\nabla_{x}u_{t}^{n}(x,y)
converges locally uniformly to
(x,y)\mapsto\nabla_{x}u_{t}(x,y)
and
X_{t}^{n}
converges to
X_{t}
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
.
Therefore,

Z_{t}^{n}
converges to
Z_{t}
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
.
By
Lemma

A.9

and (i) in
Assumption (
Regularity
)
,
we can pass to the limit in the backward ODE for
(Z_{t}^{n})_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
and deduce that, for all
t\in[t_{0},T]
,

Z_{t}=\nabla_{x}L(X_{T},Y_{0})+\int_{t}^{T}\nabla_{x}b(X_{s},\nu_{s})Z_{s}ds,

which completes the proof.
∎

A.2.
A Compactness Argument

Recalling Definition

2.18
for the space
\mathcal{R}(t_{0})
, we have the following lemma:

Lemma A.14
.

Assume that for a fixed
t_{0}\in[0,T]
, we are given a bounded sequence
(\bm{\rho}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
in
\mathcal{R}(t_{0})
. Then, there exists
\bm{\rho}\in\mathcal{R}(t_{0})
such that, up to a subsequence,

(A.31)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\|\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}=0.

Moreover,
\bm{\rho}
satisfies

(A.32)

\left\|\bm{\rho}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{0})}\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left\|\bm{\rho}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{0})}.

We need a preliminary result. To state it properly, we introduce, for any
R>0
, some cut-off function
\chi_{R}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1]
such that

(A.33)

\chi_{R}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1\quad\mbox{\rm in}&B(0,R)\\
0\quad\mbox{\rm outside}&B(0,R+1)\end{array}\right.,

with
\left\|\chi_{R}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}}\leq c
for some constant
c>0
independent of
R
.
Then, we claim:

Lemma A.15
.

Consider the functions
(\chi_{R})_{R>0}
as above. There is
C>0
such that, for all
\rho\in(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}
and all
R>0

\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\leq 1}\langle\varphi;(1-\chi_{R})\rho\rangle\leq\frac{C}{R+1}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq 1}\langle\varphi;\rho\rangle.

Proof.

A simple calculation shows that, for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}
,

\bigl\|(1-\chi_{R})\varphi\bigr\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq\frac{C}{1+R}\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}},

for some
C>0
independent from
\varphi
and
R>0
. The result easily follows.
∎

We go on with the proof of Lemma
A.14
. Be aware that throughout the proof,

\mathcal{C}^{2}_{0}
is the space of twice continuously differentiable functions
\varphi
that vanish at infinity together with their first and second derivatives, i.e.,

\lim_{R\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{|x|\geq R}\bigl(|\varphi(x)|+|\nabla_{x}\varphi(x)|+|\nabla^{2}_{x}\varphi(x)|)=0
. The space
\mathcal{C}^{2}_{0}
is equipped with
\|\cdot\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}_{b}}
.

Proof.

The proof is divided in four steps.

Step 1.
We first claim that there exists

{\bm{\rho}}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{0})^{*},w-*)
such that, up to a subsequence,

(\bm{\rho}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
\bm{\rho}
in
the following sense: for all test function
\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}_{0}^{2}
,

(A.34)

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\Bigl|\langle\rho_{t}^{n};\varphi\rangle-\langle\rho_{t};\varphi\rangle\Bigr|=0,

with
\langle\cdot;\cdot\rangle

standing for the duality bracket between
{\mathcal{C}}^{2}_{0}
and
({\mathcal{C}}^{2}_{0})^{*}
. Indeed, by Banach-Alaoglu theorem,
the ball
{\mathcal{B}}^{*}
of center
0
and radius
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\left\|\bm{\rho}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{0})}
of
(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{0})^{*}
is
compact for the weak-
*
topology. Moreover,

{\mathcal{B}}^{*}
equipped with
the weak-
*
topology
is
metrizable, with the metric

d
defined by

d(\rho,\rho^{\prime}):=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}2^{-k}|\langle\varphi_{k};\rho^{\prime}-\rho\rangle|,\qquad\rho,\rho^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{*},

where
(\varphi_{k})_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is a dense family of the unit ball

{\mathcal{B}}
of the separable Banach space
\mathcal{C}^{2}_{0}
.

Since we obviously have
d(\rho,\rho^{\prime})\leq 2\left\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{0})^{*}}\leq 2\left\|\rho^{\prime}-\rho\right\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}
, we deduce from
Definition

2.18

and the boundedness of
(\bm{\rho}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
in
\mathcal{R}(t_{0})

that the family
({\bm{\rho}}^{n})_{n\geq 1}
is uniformly continuous in time with respect to
d
, uniformly in

n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
. We can then use Arzelà-Ascoli theorem (which requires the target space to be a metric space) to infer the existence of
{\bm{\rho}}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],{\mathcal{B}}^{*})

(with
{\mathcal{B}}^{*}
being equipped with
d
)
such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}d(\rho_{t}^{n},\rho_{t})=0,

which implies
(
A.34
).

Step 2.

We now prove that
\bm{\rho}
satisfies

(A.35)

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{0},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq 1}\bigl|\langle\varphi;\rho_{t}\bigr\rangle|+\sup_{t_{2}>t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{0},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\leq 1}\frac{|\bigl\langle\varphi;\rho_{t_{2}}-\rho_{t_{1}}\bigr\rangle|}{\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}}\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left\|\bm{\rho}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{0})}.

Indeed, for all
t\in[t_{0},T]
,
t_{2}>t_{1}\in[t_{0},T]
, and
\varphi^{1},\varphi^{2}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{0}
with
\left\|\varphi^{1}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq 1
and
\left\|\varphi^{2}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\leq 1
, we get from (
A.34
) and the definition of
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{0})}
that

\displaystyle\bigl|\bigl\langle\varphi^{1};\rho_{t}\bigr\rangle\bigr|+\frac{|\bigr\langle\varphi^{2};\rho_{t_{2}}-\rho_{t_{1}}\bigr\rangle|}{\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left\{|\bigl\langle\varphi^{1};\rho^{n}_{t}\bigr\rangle|+\frac{|\bigr\langle\varphi^{2};\rho^{n}_{t_{2}}-\rho^{n}_{t_{1}}\bigr\rangle|}{\sqrt{t_{2}-t_{1}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\left\|\bm{\rho}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{0})}

and then we take the supremum over
\varphi^{1},\varphi^{2}
,
t,t_{1},t_{2}
.

Step 3.

We then justify that for all
R>0
,

(A.36)

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\bigl\|\chi_{R}(\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t})\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}=0,

with

\chi_{R}
as in (
A.33
).

To this end we fix
R>0
and take
\epsilon>0
.
Given the compact embedding
\mathcal{C}^{2}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{1}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))

(with
\overline{B}(0,R+1)
denoting the
d_{1}+d_{2}
-dimensional ball of center
0
and radius

R+1
),
we can find
\varphi^{1},\dots,\varphi^{N}\in\overline{B}_{\mathcal{C}^{2}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))}(0,1)
such that

(A.37)

\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{C}^{2}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))}(0,1)\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))}(\varphi^{i},\epsilon),

where

\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{C}^{2}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))}(0,1)

is the unit ball of

\mathcal{C}^{2}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))

and, for all integer
i=1,\dots,N
,

\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))}(\varphi^{i},\epsilon)

is the ball of

\mathcal{C}^{1}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))
of center
\varphi^{i}
and radius
\epsilon.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}
with
\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\leq 1
. We let
c_{R}:=(1+(R+1)^{2})^{-1}
so that
c_{R}\varphi
belongs to
\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{C}^{2}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))}(0,1)
. For all

1\leq i\leq N
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\biggl|\Bigl\langle c_{R}\varphi;\chi_{R}(\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t})\Bigr\rangle\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\biggl|\Bigl\langle\bigl(c_{R}\varphi-\varphi^{i}\bigr)\chi_{R};\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}\Bigr\rangle\biggr|+\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\biggl|\Bigl\langle\varphi^{i}\chi_{R};\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}\Bigr\rangle\biggr|

(A.38)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}\left\|\bm{\rho}^{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{R}(t_{0})}\left\|(c_{R}\varphi-\varphi^{i})\chi_{R}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}+\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\biggl|\Bigl\langle\varphi^{i}\chi_{R};\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}\Bigr\rangle\biggr|

with the first term on the second line following from
(
A.35
).

Now, by
(
A.37
) (and with
c
as in (
A.33
)), we can always find an integer
1\leq i\leq N
such that

\left\|(c_{R}\varphi-\varphi^{i})\chi_{R}\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{3}}\leq\frac{2c}{1+R^{3}}\left\|(c_{R}\varphi-\varphi^{i})\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}(\overline{B}(0,R+1))}\leq C\epsilon,

for a constant
C
independent of
\epsilon
. Moreover, by
(
A.34
), we can find
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that, for all
n\geq n_{0}
,

\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\biggl|\Bigl\langle\varphi^{i}\chi_{R};(\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t})\Bigr\rangle\biggr|\leq\epsilon.

Inserting the latter two displays in
(
A.38
), taking the supremum over all
\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}
with
\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2}}\leq 1
, dividing by
c_{R}
and letting
\epsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}
, this shows that (
A.36
) holds.

Step 4
.
Finally we use Lemma
A.15

to conclude.
Precisely, we show that the sequence
({\bm{\rho}}^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}}
is Cauchy in
\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*})
.
To do so, we take
\epsilon>0
. By Lemma
A.15
,
we can find
R>0
such that, for all
m,n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\bigl\|(1-\chi_{R})(\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}^{m})\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}_{2}^{2})^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\leq 1}\langle\varphi;(1-\chi_{R})\rho_{t}^{n}\rangle+\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\sup_{\varphi\in\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2},\left\|\varphi\right\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{2}}\leq 1}\langle\varphi;(1-\chi_{R})\rho_{t}^{n}\rangle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

Since the sequence
(\chi_{R}\rho^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in
\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*})
(this is the result of
Step 3
), we can find
n_{0}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
such that for all
n,m\geq n_{0}
,
we have

\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\bigl\|\chi_{R}(\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}^{m})\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

As consequence of the last two displays, we have for all
m,n\geq n_{0}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\bigl\|\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}^{m}\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\bigl\|\chi_{R}(\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}^{m})\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}+\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\bigl\|(1-\chi_{R})(\rho_{t}^{n}-\rho_{t}^{m})\bigr\|_{(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*}}\leq\epsilon.

By completeness, the sequence converges toward some
\widetilde{\bm{\rho}}\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},T],(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{2})^{*})
. Of course, for all
t\in[t_{0},T]
,
the action of

\widetilde{\rho}_{t}
on the elements of

\mathcal{C}^{2}_{0}
coincides
with the action of
\rho_{t}
. In a sense,

\widetilde{\rho}_{t}
extends
\rho_{t}
to

({\mathcal{C}}^{2}_{2})^{*}
. The bound
(
A.32
) follows by the same argument as in
Step 2
of this proof.
∎

A.3.
Pinsker Inequalities

The following result is taken from
[
6
, Theorem 2.1]
.
We feel better to restate it here as it is used repeatedly throughout the text.

Theorem A.16
.

Let
\mu
and
\nu
be two probability measures on a measurable space

{\mathcal{X}}
and
\varphi
be a non-negative valued measurable function
defined on
{\mathcal{X}}
. Then,

(A.39)

\displaystyle\bigl\|\varphi\bigl(\mu-\nu\bigr)\bigr\|_{\rm TV}\leq\biggl(\frac{3}{2}+\log\int_{\mathcal{X}}e^{2\varphi(x)}d\nu(x)\biggr)\biggl(\sqrt{{\mathcal{E}}(\mu|\nu)}+\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{E}}(\mu|\nu)\biggr),

(A.40)

\displaystyle\bigl\|\varphi\bigl(\mu-\nu\bigr)\bigr\|_{\rm TV}\leq\sqrt{2}\biggl(1+\log\int_{\mathcal{X}}e^{2\varphi^{2}(x)}d\nu(x)\biggr)^{1/2}\sqrt{{\mathcal{E}}(\mu|\nu)}.

Above,
\varphi(\mu-\nu)
is a shorthand notation for the signed measure

\varphi\mu-\varphi\nu
,
\|\varphi\bigl(\mu-\nu\bigr)\|_{\rm TV}
is its total variation norm and

{\mathcal{E}}(\mu|\nu)
denotes the relative entropy
of
\mu
with respect to
\nu
.
